...broad teeth; and has a face that is handsome and well-colored. <note>This physical description refers to the "First House," which represents the body and personality of the person asking the question.</note>
In the second <term>House</term> <note>The house of personal finances and possessions.</note> it signifies riches and many gains, but great expenses and various changes to one's status and condition; stolen or lost items are recovered and returned. However, if the question concerns a thief, it declares that he has fled away. In the third <term>House</term> <note>The house of siblings, short journeys, and communication.</note> *Joy* <note>original: "Latitia", the Latin name for this geomantic figure</note> shows brothers to be of good character, though they may have short lives; journeys will be pleasant and comfortable; and it indicates men of good credit and faith. In the fourth <term>House</term> <note>The house of the home, family, and the father.</note> it signifies happy inheritances and possessions, and a father who is noble and honored with the dignity of some princely office. It shows that treasure is located in the place being asked about, though it is of less value than expected, and ensures it will be found. In the fifth <term>House</term> <note>The house of children, creativity, and pleasure.</note> it gives obedient children gifted with good manners, who will provide the greatest joy and comfort in one's old age. It signifies that a pregnant woman will give birth to a daughter, indicates honorable embassies, and declares that rumors and news are entirely true, leaving a good and lasting reputation after death. In the sixth <term>House</term> <note>The house of health, illness, and service.</note> it shows the sick will recover and denotes good servants as well as profitable livestock and animals. In the seventh <term>House</term> <note>The house of marriage, partnerships, and legal matters.</note> *Joy* <note>original: "Latitia"</note> grants a wife who is fair, beautiful, and young; it overcomes conflicts and disputes, turning the outcome into love. In the eighth <term>House</term> <note>The house of death, taxes, and the assets of others.</note> *Joy* <note>original: "Latitia"</note> provides legacies and possessions, and a significant inheritance from a wife. If a question is asked about a person's condition, it signifies they are alive and predicts an honest, quiet, and peaceful kind of death. In the ninth <term>House</term> <note>The house of religion, philosophy, and long-distance travel.</note> *Joy* <note>original: "Latitia"</note> signifies very few journeys; for those who do travel, their trips are either for the business and embassies of Princes or pilgrimages to fulfill holy vows. It shows a person to be of good religious standing, of moderate knowledge, and one who easily understands things with natural talent. In the tenth <term>House</term> <note>The house of career, social status, and government.</note>, it raises Kings and Princes to honor and great renown, making them famous for maintaining peace during their reigns; it indicates judges who are cruel and severe <note>In the 17th century, "cruel" could imply a person who is strictly rigorous and unbending in their duty.</note>; honorable offices and magistracy; and points to matters concerning church affairs, schools, or the

<margin>The Maid</margin>
*The Girl* <note>original: "Puella", a geomantic figure associated with the planet Venus and the zodiac sign Libra. It is generally favorable for matters of love and harmony.</note> in the first <term>House</term> <note>The house of the self, personality, and physical appearance.</note> signifies a person of a short life, weak constitution of body, middle stature, slightly plump but attractive, delicate and fond of luxury, and someone who will encounter many troubles and dangers in their lifetime due to the love of women. In the second <term>House</term> <note>The house of personal finances and possessions.</note> it neither increases wealth nor decreases poverty; it indicates a thief has not left the city, and a stolen item has been sold or transferred. If the question concerns treasure in a place, it is resolved that there is none. In the third <term>House</term> <note>The house of siblings, short journeys, and communication.</note> *The Girl* signifies more sisters than brothers, and increases and continues good friendship and harmony among them; it indicates journeys to be pleasant and joyful, and people of good character. In the fourth <term>House</term> <note>The house of the home, land, and the father.</note> *The Girl* signifies a very small inheritance and a father who will not live long, but it makes the fields fertile with good crops. In the fifth <term>House</term> <note>The house of children and pleasure.</note> a pregnant woman is signaled to bring forth a baby girl; it indicates no formal embassies, causes much interaction with women, and suggests some office or position may be obtained through them. *The Girl* in the sixth <term>House</term> <note>The house of health and service.</note> signifies great weakness for the sick person, but ensures they will recover shortly; it shows a physician to be both unlearned and unskilled, but one who is highly regarded by the common people; it brings good servants, maidservants, livestock, and animals. In the seventh <term>House</term> <note>The house of marriage and partnerships.</note> *The Girl* gives a wife who is fair, beautiful, and pleasant, maintaining a peaceful and quiet life with her husband, though she will be very lustful and desired by many men; it indicates no legal suits or controversies pending before a judge, but some arguments and squabbles among the common people, which will be easily resolved and ended. [The se]venth <term>House</term> <note>The house of marriage, partnerships, and open enemies.</note> *Loss* <note>original: "Amissio"</note> giveth an adulterous wife, and one who contradicts her husband with continual contention; nevertheless she shall not live long; and it causeth contentions to be ended. In the twelfth <term>House</term> <note>The house of secrets, self-undoing, and imprisonment.</note> *Loss* destroyeth all enemies, detaineth long in prison, but preserveth from dangers.

In the third <term>House</term> <note>The house of siblings, communication, and short travels.</note> he giveth various journeys with various success, and signifieth good faith and constancy. In the fourth <term>House</term> <note>The house of the home, family, and land.</note> *Conjunction* sheweth a mean <note>Meaning average or moderate.</note> Patrimony; causeth a Father to <unclear>be</unclear> honest, of good report, and of good understanding. In the fifth <term>House</term> <note>The house of children, creativity, and pleasure.</note> he giveth Children of subtile ingenuity and wit, sheweth a woman pregnant to have a male-child, and raiseth men to honours by their own meer proper wit and ingenuity, and disperseth their fame and credit far abroad; and also signifies news and rumours to be true. In the eleventh <term>House</term> <note>The house of friends and hopes.</note> **White** maketh dissembling and false friends; causeth love and favor to be inconstant. **White** in the twelfth <term>House</term> <note>The house of hidden enemies and imprisonment.</note> denoteth vile, impotent and <term>rustical</term> <note>Unrefined, lowly, or related to the countryside.</note> enemies; sheweth such as are in prison shall not escape, and signifies a great many and various troubles and <term>discommodities</term> <note>Hardships or disadvantages.</note> of ones life.

In the tenth House, he signifies glorious princes, great and magnificent judges, high offices, and profitable leadership positions <note>original: *Magiſtracy*</note>. In the eleventh House, he causes one to have many friends and to be loved by everyone. In the twelfth House, the **Dragon’s Head** signifies that men will have many enemies and many women; he keeps the imprisoned in detention and punishes them harshly.

<meta>This page transitions from the specific descriptions of geomantic figures (the previous page ended while discussing the Dragon’s Head) to the general rules for interpreting a geomantic chart. It focuses on how to synthesize the meaning of various "Houses" and "Figures" to reach a final conclusion.</meta>

<page-num>31</page-num>

### Questions to be proposed, regarding or concerning any matter or thing whatsoever.

But now, in the manner of proceeding to a judgment, <note>In geomancy, a "judgment" is the final interpretation or answer derived from the arrangement of the figures.</note> you must especially observe this: Whenever any question is proposed to you that falls within the domain of any of the Houses, <note>Geomancy, like astrology, divides life into twelve "Houses," each governing specific areas like wealth, health, or career.</note> you shall not only provide an answer based on the figure found in that specific House; but by observing and diligently considering all the figures, and the **Index** <note>The "Index" (or *Index*) is a specific figure in a geomantic chart used to help synthesize the reading and point toward the final outcome.</note> itself within two Houses, you shall establish the foundation of your judgment. 

You shall therefore consider the figure of the thing **quesited** <term>quesited: from the Latin *quaesitum*, meaning the person, object, or matter that is the subject of the inquiry.</term> or asked after. If that figure appears again (multiplies itself) in other places within the chart, you should allow those other positions to inform your judgment as well. 

For example: if a question is asked regarding the second House <note>The house governing personal property and riches.</note> concerning a **thief**, and the figure found in the second House is also found in the sixth House <note>The house governing servants, laborers, and domestic staff.</note>, it reveals that the thief is someone from one’s own household or one of their servants. You shall judge and consider all other matters in this same manner; for this entire Art consists of the **commixtures** <note>original: *commixtures*; the blending or combining of the distinct characteristics and qualities of the geomantic figures.</note> of the figures and their various natures. Whoever practices this correctly will always be able to provide the most true and certain judgments upon every individual matter, whatever it may be.

<summary>This page explains the fundamental method of geomantic interpretation, emphasizing that a practitioner must look at how a figure "multiplies" across different houses to find specific details, such as identifying a thief as a servant.</summary>

# Of OCCULT PHILOSOPHY,
### OR
# Of MAGICAL CEREMONIES:
### The Fourth Book.
## Written by Henry Cornelius Agrippa.

In our Books of Occult Philosophy <note>Agrippa refers here to his massive three-volume work, *De Occulta Philosophia libri tres* (1533), which synthesized the magical traditions of the Renaissance.</note>, we have not so briefly <note>original: "compendiously"</note> as extensively declared the principles, foundations, and reasons of **Magic** <term>Magick: In this historical context, "Magic" refers to the study of the hidden (occult) laws of nature and the divine to produce physical effects or gain knowledge.</term> itself, and in what manner its experiments are to be chosen, selected, and combined to produce many wonderful effects. 

However, because in those books these matters are treated more **theoretically** than **practically**; and because some topics are not handled completely and fully, while others are presented very figuratively—almost like mysterious riddles <note>original: "Enigmatically"</note> and obscure puzzles—as they are those things which we have reached through great study, diligence, and very careful searching and investigation, and are previously set forth in a...

<header>The fourth book.</header>

...in a more crude and unpolished manner. Therefore, in this book—which we have composed as a **supplement** and **key** to our other books on Occult Philosophy and all magical operations—we will provide you with the principles of holy and pure truth, an unshakeable and irresistible magical discipline, and the most delightful and pleasant experiments of the sacred deities. Just as reading our other books on Occult Philosophy might make you deeply desire this knowledge, by reading this book, you shall truly succeed. For this reason, let silence keep these secrets within the hidden chambers of your religious heart, and hide them with constant **secrecy** <note>original: "Taciturnity"</note>.

Therefore, it must be understood that the names of the **intelligent governors** <note>original: "intelligent presidents"</note> of each planet are determined in this way: by gathering letters from the "figure of the world" <note>The "figure of the world" refers to an astrological chart or horoscope set for a specific moment.</note>, starting from the rising of the planet’s body, following the order of the zodiac signs through the various degrees; and from those degrees, based on the aspects of the planet itself, with the calculation beginning from the degree of the **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The degree of the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the chart.</term>. 

In the same way, the names of the **princes of evil spirits** are formed; they are identified under all the planetary governors in a reverse order—the projection being made against the order of the zodiac signs, beginning from the start of the **Seventh House** <term>Seventh House: The house in an astrological chart directly opposite the ascendant, traditionally associated with "others" and open enemies.</term>. 

Now, the name of the **supreme and highest intelligence**—which many believe to be the **Soul of the World** <note>original: "anima mundi"</note>—is gathered from the four **Cardinal points** <term>Cardinal points: The four most powerful angles in an astrological chart: the Ascendant, Descendant, Midheaven, and Imum Coeli (Bottom of the Sky).</term> of the world's figure, following the method already described. In the opposite and contrary way, the name of the **Great Spirit** <note>original: "Dæmon"</note>, or evil spirit, is identified through the four **cadent houses** <term>Cadent: The houses (3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th) that "fall away" from the powerful cardinal angles; they were often considered weaker or more hidden.</term>. 

In a similar manner, you shall understand the names of the great governing spirits ruling the **Air** from the four angles of the **succedant houses** <term>Succedant: The houses (2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th) that follow the cardinal houses.</term>. To obtain the names of good spirits, the calculation follows the order of the zodiac signs, starting from the degree of the ascendant; to find the names of evil spirits, one works in the...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for "extracting" or calculating the names of spirits from magical tables. While the previous page focused on using astrological charts (the "figure of the world"), this page explains how the names of evil spirits are derived from the names of good spirits based on their hierarchical rank.</meta>

<page-num>34</page-num>
<header>of Occult Philosophy,</header>

...in the opposite way.

You must also observe that the names of the evil spirits are extracted <note>In this context, "extracted" refers to a numerological or alphabetical process of deriving a new name from an existing one using a grid or table.</note> as well from the names of the good spirits as from the evil. However, if we enter the table with the name of a good spirit of the **second order** <term>Second Order: A middle rank in the celestial hierarchy, often associated with planetary governors.</term>, the name of the evil spirit shall be extracted from the order of the **Princes and Governors**. But if we enter the table with the name of a good spirit of the third order, or with the name of an evil spirit who is a Governor, regardless of how they are extracted—whether by this table or from an astrological chart <note>original: "celestial figure"</note>—the names that result shall be the names of evil spirits who are **Ministers** of the inferior order.

It is further to be noted that whenever we enter this table with the good spirits of the second order, the names extracted are of the second order; and if under them we extract the name of an evil spirit, he belongs to the superior order of the Governors. The same rule applies if we enter with the name of an evil spirit of the superior order. If, therefore, we enter this table with the names of the spirits of the third order, or with the names of the ministering spirits—whether they are good or evil—the names extracted shall be the names of the ministering spirits of the inferior order.

But many Magicians of significant authority believe that tables of this kind should be expanded using **Latin letters**. In this way, using these same tables, the name of any spirit (whether good or evil) might be found by using the name of their specific **office** <note>The "office" refers to the spirit's specific job or area of influence, such as "healing" or "protection."</note> or the "effect" they produce. This is done by the same method described above: by looking up the name of the office or effect in the column of letters, in its own line, under its own corresponding star. *Trismegistus* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, a legendary Hellenistic figure perceived as the author of the Hermetic Corpus and a foundational teacher of magic and alchemy.</note> is a great authority on this practice; he provided this kind of calculation using **Egyptian letters**. These methods may also be applied to the letters of other languages, following the same logic assigned to the zodiac signs; for truly, he is the only person among all those who have written on the subject who has mastered the way of obtaining the true names of spirits.

# The Fourth Book.

<sig>F 2</sig>

->Therefore, the efficacy, secrecy, and power regarding the manner in which the sacred names of spirits are truly and correctly discovered lies in the arrangement of the **vowels**.<-

These vowels form the name of a spirit, and through them, the true name and the "right word" are established. This art is perfected and executed in the following way: first, we must take care in placing the vowels of the letters found through the calculation of the **celestial figure** <note>original: "figura coelestis"; an astrological chart or horoscope set for a specific moment.</note> to find the names of the spirits of the second order, known as **Presidents and Governors**. For good spirits, this is achieved by considering the stars that constitute the letters and placing them in their proper order.

First, the degree of the **Eleventh House** <term>Eleventh House: In traditional astrology, this house is called "Agathodaemon" (the Good Spirit) and is associated with luck, hope, and celestial assistance.</term> should be subtracted from the degree of the star that is first in order. The remainder should then be **projected** <note>In astrological mathematics, "projecting" means counting a specific number of degrees starting from a certain point on the zodiac wheel.</note> from the degree of the **ascendant** <term>Ascendant: The degree of the zodiac sign that is rising over the eastern horizon at the moment for which the chart is cast.</term>. Where that number ends, there is the "part" or location of the vowel for the first letter. Begin calculating the vowels of these letters according to their number and order; the vowel that falls in the place of the star which is first in order is the vowel assigned to the first letter.

Afterward, you shall find the "part" of the second letter by subtracting the degree of the star which is second in order from the first star; the remainder is then "cast" from the **ascendant**. This is the point from which you begin the calculation of the vowels; the vowel that falls upon the second star is the vowel of the second letter. Consequently, you may always search out the vowels of the following letters by subtracting the degree of the subsequent star from the degree of the star immediately preceding it.

All calculations and numerations for the names of good spirits should be made according to the **succession of the signs** <note>The standard chronological order of the zodiac signs (Aries, Taurus, Gemini, etc.).</note>. When calculating the names of evil spirits, whereas the degree of the **Eleventh House** was used for good spirits, you must instead use the degree of the **Twelfth House** <term>Twelfth House: In traditional astrology, this house is called "Cacodaemon" (the Evil Spirit) and is associated with hidden enemies, fears, and self-undoing.</term>. All numerations and calculations may be made with the succession of the signs, taking the beginning from the

<meta>catchword: degree</meta>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining the names of spirits. While the previous page (numbered 35 in the previous session) discussed using the Eleventh House and Ascendant for the spirits of the second order, this page (marked 26 in the OCR) explains how to use "tables" and the "Alphabet" to derive the specific vowels that give those names their phonetic and spiritual power. It also introduces the "Five Places" of a birth chart for finding personal guardian spirits.</meta>

<page-num>26</page-num>

# Of Occult Philosophy

...degree of the **Tenth House** <term>Tenth House: In astrology, the Midheaven (Medium Coeli), representing one's social standing, authority, and the highest point of the sky at a given moment.</term>.

However, in all extractions performed using tables, the vowels are arranged in a different manner. First, one must take the specific number of letters that make up the name itself. This number is counted starting from the beginning of the column of the first letter—or from where the name is being extracted. The letter where this count ends is then compared to the first letter of the extracted name by calculating the distance between them according to the standard order of the **Alphabet**.

The number of that distance is then **projected** <note>In astrological and magical mathematics, "projecting" refers to counting a specific number of units (degrees or letters) starting from a designated point.</note> from the beginning of its respective column. Where that count ends, we find the location of the first vowel. From that point, you shall calculate the vowels themselves according to their own number and order within that same column. The vowel that lands on the first letter of a name is the one assigned to that name.

You will find the subsequent vowels by calculating the distance from the preceding vowel to the following one, following the sequence of the Alphabet. This distance is then counted from the start of its own column; where the count stops, you have found the location of the sought-after vowel. From there, you must calculate the vowels as described above. The vowels that land on their corresponding letters are then assigned to them. If a vowel happens to land upon another vowel, the first must give way to the second. Note that this specific procedure is intended only for **good spirits**.

For **evil spirits**, you may follow the same procedure, with one exception: the calculations must be done in a contrary and backward order—opposite to the sequence of the Alphabet and the order of the columns (that is to say, in **ascending** <note>original: "in ascending"; meaning to count from the bottom of a list to the top, or from Z to A.</note> order).

The method for finding the names of **good Angels** and of every individual person—which we taught in our *Third Book of Occult Philosophy* <note>Referring to Henry Cornelius Agrippa’s primary work, *De Occulta Philosophia libri III* (1533), specifically the chapters on the genius or daemon of a person.</note>—is of significant authority and is built upon a strong foundation. However, we will now provide you with other methods supported by sound reasoning. One of these methods involves taking the five significant places from the **figure of the nativity** <term>Figure of the Nativity: A birth chart or horoscope calculated for the exact moment and location of a person’s birth.</term>...

<summary>This page describes the mathematical and alphabetical "extraction" process used to find the vowels of a spirit's name. It distinguishes between the forward-moving calculations for good spirits and the backward, "ascending" calculations used for evil spirits.</summary>

### The fourth book.

...of the <term>Hyleg</term> <note>original: "Hylech"; the Hyleg is the planet or point in a birth chart that acts as the "giver of life" and is used to determine a person's vitality and lifespan.</note>: once this is identified, the characters of the letters are projected in their specific order and number starting from the beginning of **Aries**; and those letters which fall upon the degrees of the mentioned places—disposed and aspected according to their order and dignity—form the name of an Angel. There is also another method, in which they use the <term>Almuten</term> <note>original: "Almutel"; the planet that holds the greatest influence or "dignity" over a specific point or house in a horoscope.</note>, which refers to the ruling and governing stars over the five places mentioned previously. In this case, the projection is made starting from the degree of the ascendant; this is done by gathering the letters that fall upon the Almuten. When these letters are placed in order according to their dignity, they form the name of an Angel. Furthermore, there is another method used and very highly regarded by the <term>Egyptians</term> <note>Agrippa often refers to "Egyptians" as the ancient keepers of Hermetic and astrological wisdom.</note>, which involves making the calculation from the degree of the ascendant and gathering the letters according to the Almuten of the **Eleventh House**. The Egyptians call this house a **good spirit** <note>original: "Dæmon"; in traditional astrology, the 11th House is the "House of the Good Spirit" (Bona Fortuna), associated with luck and protection.</note>. Once these are arranged according to their dignities, the names of the Angels are established.

Now, the names of the evil Angels are discovered in a similar way, except that the projections must be performed in the direction opposite to the natural order of the signs of the zodiac. Thus, while we calculate from the beginning of **Aries** when seeking the names of good spirits,

# Of Occult Philosophy,

Now these <term>celestial characters</term> <note>These are magical sigils or symbols believed to represent the spiritual essence of a star or planet.</note> consist of lines and heads. The heads are fixed according to the fixed <term>magnitudes</term> <note>original: "magnitudes"; a system used since antiquity to rank stars by their brightness, with the first magnitude being the brightest.</note> of the stars, to which the planets are also categorized. 

The first magnitude corresponds to a star, with the **Sun**, or a **Cross**. The second magnitude, with **Jupiter**, uses a **circular point**. The third corresponds with **Saturn** and uses a **semicircle** or a **triangle**—which may be curved, rounded, or acute. The fourth magnitude, associated with **Mars**, uses a **small stroke** that cuts through a line, which may be square, straight, or slanted. The fifth, with **Venus** and **Mercury**, uses a **small stroke or a point with a tail**, either pointing up or down. The sixth magnitude, with the **Moon**, uses a **blackened point**. 

All of these you may see in the following table. Once the "heads" are placed according to the position of the stars in the chart of the heavens, the lines are then drawn out according to how their natures agree or align. You should understand this to be the method for the fixed stars. However, when creating symbols for the **Planets**, the lines are drawn out after the heads are positioned according to the planets' own movements and relationships with one another.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for constructing magical symbols. ### The Fourth Book

An <term>image</term> <note>In this context, a "celestial image" refers to a constellation or a specific astrological figure.</note> rising in any degree or <term>face</term> <note>original: "facies"; a ten-degree subdivision of a zodiac sign, often called a decan.</note> of a sign consists of stars of the same brightness and nature. The number of these stars is arranged according to their specific place and order, and lines are then drawn to resemble the shape of the figure they represent, as clearly as can be managed.

However, the <term>characters</term> <note>Magical sigils or symbols believed to hold the power of a specific entity.</note> derived from the name of a spirit are created using the table that follows. Every letter is assigned the name that corresponds to it in the table. While this may seem easy to those who grasp the concept, it actually involves considerable difficulty—specifically, knowing which figure or letter to choose when a letter of a name falls upon a specific line of letters or figures. 

This may be determined in the following way: if a letter falls upon the line of letters, consider its position in the order of the name (for example, whether it is the second or third letter). Then, count how many letters the name contains in total (such as five or seven). Multiply these two numbers together, and then triple the product. Count this final total out from the beginning of the alphabet; the letter upon which that number falls is the one that should be used for the character of that spirit. 

But if any letter of a name falls on the line of figures, it is calculated as follows: take the number representing the letter’s position in the name and multiply it by the number representing its position in the alphabet. Add these together and divide the sum by nine. The remainder indicates the figure or number to be used in the character. This can be expressed as either a geometric shape or an <term>arithmetical figure</term> <note>A standard number.</note>; regardless, it must not exceed the number nine or have more than nine angles.

<summary>This page explains two methods for creating magical sigils: one based on the visual arrangement of stars in a constellation, and another using a complex mathematical system to convert a spirit's name into symbolic characters via alphabetic and numerical calculations.</summary>

<keywords>astrological images, decans, spirit names, sigil construction, sacred geometry, magic squares, alphabetic calculation, numerology</keywords>

### Shapes familiar to the Spirits of the Sun.

->☉ <note>The astrological and alchemical symbol for the Sun</note><-

<margin>Matthew 14: 29, 30, 31, 32</margin>
...him; and also, that he drew *Peter* out of the water when he was in danger of drowning, and other similar acts. Finally, with these we invoke and call upon certain holy names of God; specifically, those names that signify the accomplishment of our desire and are suited to the intended effect. For example, if the goal is to overthrow enemies, we should invoke and call upon names reflecting the wrath, revenge, fear, justice, and fortitude of God. If we wish to avoid or escape some evil or danger, we then call upon the names of mercy, defense, salvation, fortitude, goodness, and other such names of God.

<margin>A prayer</margin>
Furthermore, when we pray to God to grant our desires, we should also intermix the name of some good spirit—whether just one or many—whose office <note>The specific duty or "job description" assigned to a spirit in the celestial hierarchy.</note> it is to execute our desires. Sometimes we also require an evil spirit to be restrained or compelled, and we intermingle their name as well; this is done rightly, especially if the goal is to carry out a work of "evil," such as revenge, punishment, or destruction.

<margin>Psalm 149:6; 2 Samuel 15:31, 34</margin>
Furthermore, if there is any <term>versicle: a short verse, usually from the Bible, used as a formula in ritual</term> in the Psalms or any other part of Holy Scripture that seems fitting and agreeable to our desire, it should be mingled into our prayers. 

<margin>An Oration</margin>
Now, after prayer has been made to God, it is helpful to afterwards make an <term>oration: a formal, ritualized speech or address</term> to that <term>executioner: the specific spirit or entity tasked with carrying out the requested action</term> whom we asked God to send to us in our previous prayer—whether it be one or many, or whether it be an Angel, a Star, a Soul, or any of the noble Angels. 

<margin>Book: Page:</margin>
This kind of oration should be composed according to the rules we provided in the second book of *Occult Philosophy* <note>Referring to Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa's influential 16th-century work, *De Occulta Philosophia*.</note>, where we discussed the method for composing <term>enchantments: ritual spoken formulas intended to produce a magical effect</term>.

You should also know that these kinds of <term>bonds: ritual constraints used to compel or "tie" a spirit to a task</term> have a threefold difference. The first bond is when we <term>conjure: to call upon or command a spirit through a ritual oath</term> by natural things. The second is compounded of religious mysteries—by sacraments, miracles, and things of this sort. The third is established by divine names and holy <term>sigils: symbolic icons or "signatures" representing a spirit or a divine force</term>. By these kinds of bonds, we may bind not only spirits but also all creatures whatsoever, such as animals, tempests, \*burnings, floods of water, and the force and power...

<margin>\*Fires; Envy and Malice.</margin>

<margin>
Consecration
</margin>
And now we come to treat of the **Consecrations** which men ought to make upon all instruments and things necessary to be used in this Art: and the virtue of this Consecration most chiefly consists in two things; to wit, in the power of the person consecrating, and by the virtue of the prayer by which the Consecration is made. For in the person consecrating, there is required holiness of Life, and power of sanctifying: both of which are acquired by **Dignification** <note>The process of making oneself ritually worthy through purification or status.</note> and **Initiation**. And that the person himself should with a firm and undoubted faith believe the virtue, power, and efficacy hereof. And then in the Prayer itself by which this Consecration is made, there is required the like holiness; which either solely consists in the prayer itself, as, if it be by divine inspiration ordained to this purpose, such as we have in many places of the holy Bible; or that it be hereunto instituted through the power of the Holy Spirit, in the ordination of the Church. Otherwise there is in the Prayer a **Santimony** <note>Holiness or sacredness.</note>, which is not only by itself, but by the commemoration of holy things; as, the commemoration of holy Scriptures, Histories, Works, Miracles, Effects, Graces, Promises, Sacraments and Sacramental things, and the like. Which things, by a certain similitude, do seem properly or improperly to appertain to the thing consecrated.

# The Fourth Book.

[...quired] with the signing <term>consignation | The act of marking an object with a ritual sign, such as the sign of the cross.</term> of holy Seals, and things of a similar sort, which lead to sanctification and purification <term>expiation | A ritual cleansing to remove spiritual or physical defilement.</term>; such as the sprinkling with Holy Water, anointings with holy oil, and fragrant incense-burnings <term>suffumigations | The ritual use of smoke from burning herbs or resins to purify or summon.</term> belonging to holy worship. Therefore, in every consecration, the blessing and hallowing of Water, Oil, Fire, and Incense is primarily used, performed everywhere with holy wax candles or burning lamps; for without lights, no sacrament is performed correctly.

This, therefore, is to be known and firmly observed: that if any consecration is to be made of profane things—in which there is any pollution or defilement—then an exorcising and purification of those things must precede the consecration. Once these things are made pure, they are more capable of receiving the influences of the divine virtues. 

We are also to observe that at the end of every consecration, after the prayer is rightly performed, the person consecrating ought to bless the thing being hallowed by breathing out <note>The practice of "insuflation" or breathing upon an object was a common ritual act to transmit spiritual power or the "breath of life."</note> certain words, filled with divine virtue and the power of the present consecration, while calling upon his own virtue and authority so that the rite may be more duly performed, and with an earnest and focused mind. Therefore, we will set down some examples here, so that the way to total perfection in this art may be more easily revealed to you.

<margin>
*Consecration of Water*
</margin>

Likewise, we are to call to remembrance how God made the water to be the instrument of executing his justice in the destruction of the Giants in the Great Flood <note>original: "general deluge"</note> over all the earth, and in the overthrow of the army of Pharaoh in the Red Sea; also, how God led his own people through the midst of the Sea on dry ground, and through the midst of the river Jordan; and likewise how marvelously he drew forth water out of the stony rock in the wilderness;

Beyond these, there is another rite of consecration of wonderful power and great efficacy. This falls under the category of "superstitions" <note>Here, "superstitions" refers to the practice of "over-stepping" traditional religious boundaries by borrowing or transferring official Church liturgy for private ritual use.</note>: namely, when the rite of consecration or the liturgy of any Sacrament of the Church is transferred to the object we wish to consecrate.

It should also be known that vows, offerings, and sacrifice have the power of consecration, both for the person and the object. These act as certain covenants and agreements between the divine names being invoked and the practitioner making the offering, binding the power to our desire and the intended effect. This applies when we dedicate, offer, and sacrifice using specific names or objects, such as: **Fumigations** <term>fumigations | The ritual use of incense or aromatic smoke to purify or empower a space or object.</term>, **Unctions** <term>unctions | The act of anointing with holy oils.</term>, rings, images, and looking-glasses. # The Fourth Book

Scriptures: of which we have spoken extensively in our third book of *Occult Philosophy*.

<margin>The Book of Spirits <note>original: "Liber Spirituum"</note></margin>
There exists among those magicians who most frequently employ the service of malevolent spirits a specific rite for invoking spirits using a book previously consecrated for that purpose. This is properly called *The Book of Spirits*, and we shall now speak briefly about it. This book is to be consecrated as a book of malevolent spirits, ceremoniously composed in their name and hierarchy. Within its pages, the spirits are bound by a holy oath to provide prompt and immediate obedience.

Now, this book must be made from the purest, cleanest paper that has never been used before, which many call **virgin paper** <term>virgin paper | Technically "charta virginea," this refers to parchment or paper that has never been written upon, symbolizing ritual purity and a lack of previous spiritual "imprints."</term>. This book must be inscribed in the following manner: on the left side, place the image of the spirit, and on the right side, place its **character** <term>character | A magical symbol, seal, or sigil unique to a specific spirit that acts as its signature or focal point for invocation.</term>. Above these, place the Oath containing the spirit's name, its rank and station, and its specific office and power. Although many people compose this book differently by omitting the characters or images, it is more effective not to neglect anything that contributes to the goal.

Furthermore, one must observe the specific circumstances of place, time, and hour, according to the stars under which these spirits fall and with which they are aligned, applying their proper position, ritual, and order.

Once the book is written and well-bound, it should be decorated, embellished, and kept secure with markers and seals. This prevents the book from accidentally opening in an unintended place after its consecration, which could endanger the practitioner. Moreover, this book must be kept with the greatest possible reverence; for a disrespectful mind, along with pollution or profanation, causes the book to lose its power.

Now that this sacred book has been composed according to the method already described, we must proceed to its consecration in one of two ways. The first way is this: all and each of the spirits recorded in the book are called to the **Circle** <term>Circle | A protective ritual boundary drawn on the floor, designed to protect the magician and contain the energy of summoned spirits.</term>, following the established rites and order...

There, after first opening the book, let it be consecrated according to the rites and ways we have previously declared concerning Consecration. For example, using the Old Testament, one might mention the dreams of *Jacob*, *Joseph*, *Pharaoh*, *Daniel*, and *Nebuchadnezzar*. If, when the practitioner goes to sleep, it is with a firm intention and they have properly prepared themselves, let them pray devoutly; without doubt, these prayers will produce a powerful effect.

# Of Occult Philosophy,

Within the **Circle** itself, the general divine names are to be written, along with those things which provide us with defense. Alongside these, include those divine names that rule over the specific **Planet** being worked with, and the **Offices** <note>The "offices" refer to the specific duties, powers, or areas of influence assigned to a particular spirit.</note> of the **Spirit** itself. One should also write the names of the **good Spirits** who hold authority and are able to bind and compel the specific Spirit we intend to summon. 

If we wish to further fortify and strengthen our Circle, we may add **Characters** <term>Characters | In this context, these are "sigils" or symbolic signatures of spirits and planetary forces.</term> and **Pentacles** <term>Pentacles | Consecrated geometric symbols, often drawn on parchment or engraved on metal, used as protective talismans and tools of authority over spirits.</term> that agree with the nature of the work. Furthermore, if we choose, we may frame an angular figure—either inside or outside the Circle—inscribed with appropriate numbers that are harmonious with our objective. These numbers and figures should be chosen according to the principles of numerology and geometry, which are discussed extensively in the second book of *Occult Philosophy*. 

Furthermore, the practitioner must be provided with lights, perfumes, <term>unguents | Holy oils or ointments applied to the body or ritual tools.</term> and medicines, all compounded according to the nature of the specific Planet and Spirit. These items work in two ways: first, they harmonize with the Spirit through their natural and celestial **virtue** <note>In historical magic, "virtue" refers to an inherent power or spiritual property residing within a physical substance.</note>; and second, they are presented to the Spirit as a form of religious and ritual honor. 

The practitioner must also be furnished with holy and consecrated items, which are necessary both for the protection of the **Invocant** <note>The person performing the summoning.</note> and their companions, and for serving as "bonds" to restrain and command the Spirits. These items include holy papers, **Lamens** <term>Lamens | Metal plates or amulets worn around the neck, engraved with magical symbols to protect the wearer and command spirits.</term>, pictures, pentacles, swords, scepters, and garments made of appropriate materials and colors. 

When all these things are prepared, and the Master and his companions are standing within the Circle, he shall in the <unclear>first</unclear> place <note>The word "first" is partially faded in the original due to staining.</note> **consecrate** <term>consecrate | To make a thing holy or dedicated to a divine purpose through ritual purification.</term> the Circle and all the tools he intends to use. Once this is performed with a respectful gesture and a solemn expression, he should begin to pray with a loud voice in the following manner: First, he shall offer an oration to God, and then he shall entreat the assistance of the good Spirits. If he wishes to read any prayers, Psalms, or Gospels for his protection, these should be recited first. After these prayers and orations are finished, he may then begin to **invocate** <term>invocate | To call upon a higher power or spirit for assistance or presence.</term> the Spirit he desires with a gentle and loving **Enchantment**, directed toward all the corners of the world, while commemorating the Spirit's own autho- <meta>catchword: rity</meta>

And after all the courses are finished, then cease a little; and if any **Spirit** shall appear, let the **Invocant** <note>The person performing the ritual summoning.</note> turn himself towards the Spirit, and courteously receive him, and earnestly intreating him, let him first require his name, and if he be called by any other name: and then proceeding further, let him ask him whatsoever he will: and if in any thing the Spirit shall show himself obstinate or lying, let him be bound by convenient conjurations: and if you doubt of any lie, make without the **Circle** with the **consecrated Sword**, the figure of a triangle or *Pentagone* <note>original: "Pentagone"; a five-sided figure, likely a pentagram, used here as a cage or witness-box for the spirit.</note>, and compel the Spirit to enter into it: and if thou receivest any promise which thou wouldst have to be confirmed with an **Oath**, let him stretch the sword out of the Circle, and swear the Spirit, by laying his hand upon the Sword. 

Then having obtained of the Spirit that which you desire, or are otherwise contented, license him to depart with courteous words, giving command unto him, that he do no hurt: and if he will not depart, compel him by powerful conjurations; and if need require, expel him by **Exorcismes**, and by making contrary **fumigations** <term>fumigations | The ritual burning of incense or herbs; "contrary" fumigations use foul-smelling substances to drive away unwanted spirits.</term>. And when he is departed, go not out of the **Circle**, but make a stay, making prayer, and giving of thanks unto God and the good **Angels**, and also praying for your defence and conservation <note>Protection and safety.</note>: and then all those things being orderly performed, you may depart.

...passions, and find joy in the company of men, and freely live among them; others dwell in the woods and deserts; some delight in the company of various domestic animals and wild beasts; and others live around springs and meadows. Therefore, whoever wishes to call upon these kinds of spirits in the places where they reside should do so with fragrant perfumes and with sweet sounds and musical instruments specifically composed for the task, using songs, enchantments, and pleasant verses, along with praises and promises.

But those who are stubborn and refuse to yield to these things are to be compelled with threats, denunciations, curses, trickery, and insults—and especially by threatening to expel them from the places where they usually spend their time.

Furthermore, if necessary, you may resort to using *exorcisms*; but the most important thing to be observed is a steady mind and a boldness that is free and detached from fear.

Lastly, when you wish to invoke these kinds of spirits, you should prepare a table in the place of invocation, covered with clean linen. Upon it, you shall place fresh bread and running water or milk in new earthenware vessels, along with new knives. You shall also build a fire where a perfume <note>In this context, "perfume" refers to ritual incense or aromatic smoke burnt to attract spirits.</note> shall be made. The <term>Invocant | The person performing the summoning ritual.</term> should go to the head of the table, and seats for the spirits should be placed around it as you see fit; and once the spirits are called, you shall invite them to eat and drink. However, if you happen to fear any evil spirit, then draw a **circle** about the table, and let the part of the table where the invocant sits be within the **circle**, while the rest of the table remains outside the **circle**.

In our third book of *Occult Philosophy* <note>Agrippa refers here to his major three-volume work, *De Occulta Philosophia*, which covers the natural, celestial, and divine realms of magic.</note>, we have taught how and by what means the *Soul* is joined to the *Body*; and what happens to the *Soul* after death.

...affinity alluring them; such are the souls of **noxious men** <term>Noxious men | Individuals who were wicked, harmful, or died in a state of moral corruption.</term>, who have violently relinquished their bodies, and souls lacking a proper burial, which still wander in a liquid and turbulent spirit around their dead carcasses; for these souls, by the known means by which they were previously joined to their bodies, are easily drawn to them by similar vapors, liquids, and scents.

Because of this, the souls of the dead are not to be called up without blood, or by the application of some part of their remaining body.

In the raising up of these *shadows* <note>In classical and occult literature, "shadows" refers to the "shades" or ghostly remnants of the deceased.</note>, we must use perfumes <note>In this context, "perfumes" refers to ritual incense or the smoke from burning offerings.</note> made with new blood, the bones of the dead, and with flesh, eggs, milk, honey, and oil, and similar things which provide the souls with a means suitable to receive their bodies.

It must also be understood that those who desire to raise any souls of the dead ought to do so in those places where these kinds of souls are most known to be present—either due to some alliance drawing those souls back to their forsaken bodies, or some kind of past affection impressed upon them during life, drawing the soul to certain places, things, or persons; or due to the powerful nature of a place fitted and prepared to purge or punish these souls. These places are mostly known through the experience of visions, powerful incursions, apparitions, and similar supernatural signs.

Therefore, the most fitting places for these rites are **churchyards**. Even better than those are places where criminal judgments are executed <note>Refers to gallows or execution grounds.</note>. And better still are those places where there have been public slaughters of men in recent years. Furthermore, the best place of all is where some dead carcass, having met a violent death, has not yet been purified by ritual nor properly buried, and was only recently interred; for the **expiation** <term>Expiation | The ritual purification or atonement of a site to put a soul to rest.</term> of those places is also a holy rite that should be properly applied to the burial of bodies; it often prevents souls from returning to their bodies and expels them far away to the places of judgment.

<header>The fourth book.</header>

And from this, it follows that the souls of the dead are not easily raised up, except for the souls of those whom we know to be **evil**, or those who have perished by a **violent death**, and those whose bodies lack a right and proper burial.

Now, although we have discussed these types of places <note>Referring to gravesites or scenes of violence mentioned on the previous page.</note>, it will not always be safe or convenient to travel to them. Instead, it is necessary to take some **principal part of the body** that remains <term>Relict | A physical relic of the deceased, such as a bone, lock of hair, or fragment of skin.</term> to whatever place you have chosen, and there create a ritual perfume <note>In occult texts, "perfume" almost always refers to ritual incense or the smoke from burning specific offerings.</note> in the proper manner, and perform other appropriate rites.

It is also to be known that because souls are a form of *spiritual light*, artificial lights—especially if they are crafted from specific, suitable materials and compounded according to true magical rules, with the correct inscriptions of **Names and Seals**—are very effective in the raising up of departed souls.

Moreover, these physical things we have spoken of are not always sufficient to raise souls because of the **supernatural element of understanding and reason** <note>original: "extranatural portion"</note> within the soul, which is of a higher nature and known only to Heaven, the Destinies, and their power.

We must, therefore, allure these souls using supernatural and celestial powers duly administered. This is done through things that move the very "harmony of the soul," affecting the imagination as well as the rational and intellectual mind. These include **Voices, Songs, Sounds, and Enchantments**. Additionally, religious elements should be used, such as **Prayers, Conjurations, Exorcisms**, and other holy rites, which can be very conveniently applied to this purpose.

# Heptameron,
<note>Greek: "Seven Days." This title refers to the *Heptameron, or Elements of Magic*, a famous grimoire of planetary evocations traditionally attributed to the 13th-century philosopher Peter de Abano.</note>

->of Occult Philosophy,<-

<meta>This page concludes the practical instructions for necromancy—specifically the summoning of spirits of the dead—found in the "Fourth Book of Occult Philosophy" (often attributed to Agrippa). It transitions from the physical requirements of relics and incense to the spiritual "allurements" of music and prayer, ending with the introduction to the Heptameron, a famous manual of planetary magic.</meta>

<warning>The text on this page is a mirrored offset (ink transfer) from a facing page, appearing as reversed text on a blank flyleaf.</warning>

...and from this point forward, the souls of the dead are not to be summoned, except for those whom we know are still held back by a **violent death**, or those whose bodies lack their **rightful burial**. 

It is not surprising that for souls of this kind, it is possible to call them back even after some time has passed. However, it is necessary for us to choose a specific location—ideally where some **principal part of the body** <term>Relict | A physical remain of the deceased, such as a bone, lock of hair, or fragment of skin, used as a focal point for the spirit.</term> remains—and there perform a ritual fumigation <note>original: "perfume"; this refers to the ritual burning of specific herbs or incense to create an atmosphere conducive to spirits.</note> according to its natural properties, alongside other appropriate ceremonies.

It should also be understood that because souls are a form of *spiritual light*, symbolic figures—if they are crafted from specific, suitable materials and designed according to magical rules, with the correct inscriptions of **Names and Seals** <note>Magical signatures or symbols believed to hold authority over specific spirits.</note>—are highly effective in the summoning of departed souls.

Furthermore, these physical items we have mentioned are not always enough to raise a soul. This is because there is a **supernatural element of understanding and reason** <note>original: "extraordinary portion of understanding"</note> within the soul, which is of a higher nature and known only to the Heavens, the **Deities**, and their divine power. 

Therefore, we must stir these higher souls through spiritual and celestial powers, properly administered. We must use those things that appeal to the very memory of the soul—considering its dual nature as both rational and intellectual—such as the human **Voice**, **Songs**, **Musical Sounds**, and **Incantations**. Sacred elements are also used, including **Conjurations**, **Exorcisms**, and other holy rites that are well-suited to be administered for this purpose.

# Heptameron :
<term>Heptameron | A title derived from the Greek words for "seven" (hepta) and "day" (hemera), signifying a work organized around the seven days of the week and their corresponding planetary rulers.</term>
->OR,<-
## MAGICAL ELEMENTS
->OF<-
## PETER de ABANO
### PHILOSOPHER.
<note>Pietro d'Abano (c. 1250–1316) was a famous Italian physician, astrologer, and professor. Although this text was attributed to him, it was likely written in the centuries following his death.</note>

In the former book, which is the fourth book of *Agrippa* <note>Refers to Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa. The "Fourth Book of Occult Philosophy" is a famous grimoire on ceremonial magic, though scholars believe it was written by a follower rather than Agrippa himself.</note>, it is sufficiently spoken concerning Magical Ceremonies, and Initiations.

But because he seemeth to have written to the learned, and well-experienced in this Art; because he doth not specially treat of the Ceremonies, but rather speaketh of them in general, it was therefore thought good to adde hereunto the Magical Elements of *Peter de Abano*; that those who are hither- <note>The text breaks here; the catchword "to" indicates the word "hitherto" continues on the next page.</note>

<summary>This page serves as the title page and introduction to the Heptameron, presenting it as a practical guide to supplement the more general magical theories of Agrippa.</summary>

to the ignorant, and those who have not tasted of magical superstitions <note>In this historical context, "superstitions" refers to the specialized ritual practices and arcane beliefs of the occult arts.</note>, may have them in readiness, so that they may exercise themselves therein. For we see in this book, as it were, a certain introduction to magical vanity <note>The term "vanity" here likely refers to the "arts" or "illusions" of magic, or perhaps the transition from worldly to spiritual concerns.</note>; and as if they were in actual practice, they may behold the distinct functions of spirits, how they may be drawn to discourse and communication; what is to be done every day, and every hour; and how they shall be read, as if they were described syllable by syllable.

In brief, in this book are kept the principles of magical conveyances <note>Methods or techniques for directing magical power or summoning spirits.</note>. But because the greatest power is attributed to the Circles (for they are certain fortresses to keep the operators safe from evil spirits); in the first place, we will treat the composition of a Circle.

# *Of the Circle, and the composition thereof.*

The form of Circles is not always one and the same; but is usually changed according to the order of the spirits that are to be called, their places, times, days, and hours. For in making a Circle, it ought to be considered at what time of the year, what day, and what hour you make the Circle; what spirits you would call, to what star and region <note>Refers to the planetary associations and the cardinal directions (North, South, East, West) governed by specific spirits.</note> they belong, and what functions they have.

Therefore, let there be made three circles with a width of nine feet, and let them be distant from one another by a hand's breadth; and in the middle circle, first, write the name of the hour in which you do the work. In the second place, write the name of the Angel of the hour. In the third place, the Sigil <term>Sigil | A mystical symbol or "signature" used to represent and summon a specific spirit or angel.</term> of the Angel of the hour. Fourthly, the name of the Angel that rules that day in which you do the work, and the names of his ministers. In the fifth place, the name of the present time. Sixthly, the name of the spirits ruling in that part of time, and their presidents. Seventhly, the name of the head of the Sign <note>Refers to the sign of the Zodiac ruling that particular astronomical period.</note> ruling in that part of

time wherein you work. Eighthly, The name of the earth, according to that part of time wherein you work. Ninthly, and for the completing of the middle Circle, Write the name of the Sun and of the Moon, according to the said rule of time; for as the time is changed, so the names are to be altered. And in the outermost Circle, let there be drawn in the four Angles, the names of the presiding Angels of the Air, that day wherein you would do this work; to wit, the name of the King and his three Ministers. Without the Circle, in four Angles, let Pentagons <note>original: "Pentagones"</note> be made. In the inner Circle let there be written four divine names with crosses interposed in the middle of the Circle; to wit, towards the East let there be written *Alpha* <note>Greek for "The First" or "Beginning"</note>, and towards the West let there be written *Omega* <note>Greek for "The Last" or "End"</note>; and let a cross divide the middle of the Circle. When the Circle is thus finished, according to the rule now before written, you shall proceed.

## *Of the names of the hours, and the Angels ruling them.*

It is also to be known, that the Angels do rule the hours in a successive order, according to the course of the heavens, and Planets unto which they are subject; so that that Spirit which governeth the day, ruleth also the first hour of the day; the second from this governeth the second hour; the third, the third hour, and so consequently: and when seven Planets and hours have made their revolution, it returneth again to the first which ruleth the day <note>This describes the "Planetary Hours" system, where each hour is assigned a celestial ruler based on the "Chaldean Order" (Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, Moon).</note>. Therefore we shall first speak of the names of the hours.

# The Consecrations and Benedictions: and first of the Benediction of the Circle.

When the Circle is rightly <note>original: "ritely"</note> perfected, sprinkle the same with holy or purging water, and say,

> *Thou shalt purge me with hyssop, O Lord, and I shall be clean: Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow.* <note>This is a translation of the Latin "Asperges me," a traditional verse from Psalm 51 used in liturgical and magical cleansing rituals.</note>

## The Blessing <note>original: "Benediction"</note> of the perfumes.

The God of Abraham, God of Isaac, God of Jacob, bless here these kinds of creatures <note>In historical theology, "creatures" refers to any created thing, such as the herbs or resins used for incense</note>, that they may fill up the power and virtue of their odors; so that neither the enemy, nor any false imagination <note>Likely referring to deceptive spirits or mental delusions</note>, may be able to enter into them: through our Lord Jesus Christ, etc. Then let them be sprinkled with holy water.

## The Exorcism of the fire upon which the perfumes are to be put.

The fire which is to be used for fumigations <note>original: "suffumigations" — the ritual act of burning incense to produce smoke</note>, is to be in a new vessel of earth <note>clay or ceramic</note> or iron; and let it be exorcised after this manner. I exorcise thee, O thou creature of fire, by him by whom all things are made, that forthwith thou cast away every phantom <note>original: "phantaſme"</note> from thee, that it shall not be able to do any hurt in any thing. Then say, Bless, O Lord, this creature of fire, and sanctify it, that it may be blessed to set forth the praise of thy holy name, that no hurt may come to the Practitioners <note>original: "Exerciſers"</note> or Spectators: through our Lord Jesus Christ, etc.

## Of the Garment and Pentacle.

Let it be a Priest’s Garment, if it can be: but if it cannot be had, let it be of linen, and clean. Then take this Pentacle <note>A ritual talisman, usually a disc or piece of parchment inscribed with sacred symbols</note> made in the day and hour of Mercury, the Moon increasing <note>the waxing moon</note>, written in parchment made of a kid’s <note>young goat</note> skin. But first let there be said over it the Mass of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Maſs of the holy Ghost"</note>, and let it be sprinkled with water of baptism.

# Of the manner of working.

**L**et the *Moon* be increasing and equal <note>Likely referring to the Moon being in a balanced or harmonious astrological aspect</note>, if it may then be done, and let her not be combust <note>In astrology, "combust" means a planet is too close to the Sun, which was thought to weaken its power</note>.

The Operator ought to be clean and purified by the space of nine days before the beginning of the work, and to be confessed, and receive the holy Communion. Let him have ready the perfume <note>Incense</note> appropriated to the day wherein he would perform the work. He ought also to have holy water from a Priest, and a new earthen vessel with fire, a Vesture and Pentacle; and let all these things be rightly and duly consecrated and prepared. Let one of the servants carry the earthen vessel full of fire, and the perfumes, and let another bear the book, another the Garment and Pentacle, and let the master carry the Sword; over which there must be said one mass of the Holy Ghost; and on the middle of the Sword, let there be written this name *Agla* <note>original: "Agla" — A Hebrew acronym (Notarikon) for "Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai," meaning "Thou art mighty forever, O Lord"</note> †, and on the other side thereof, this name † *On* <note>original: "On" — A divine name used in medieval magic, often signifying "Being" or "Strength"</note> †. And as he goeth to the consecrated place, let him continually read Litanies, the servants answering. And when he cometh to the place where he will erect the Circle, let him draw the lines of the Circle, as we have before taught: and after he hath made it, let him sprinkle the Circle with holy water, saying, <margin>Thou shalt sprinkle me, O Lord, and I shall be clean; thou shalt wash me, and I shall be whiter than snow. <note>original: "Asperges me Domine, &c." This is a traditional purification rite based on Psalm 51:7.</note></margin> *Asperges me Domine, &c.*

The Master therefore ought to be purified with fasting, chastity, and abstinency from all luxury the space of three whole days before the day of the operation. And on the day that he would do the work, being clothed with pure garments, and furnished with Pentacles, Perfumes, and other things necessary hereunto, let him enter the Circle, and call the Angels from the four parts of the world, which do govern the seven Planets the seven days of the week, Colours and Metals; whose name you shall see in their places. And with bended knees invocating the said Angels particularly, let him say, *O you angels mentioned above, be helpers to my request, and...* <note>original: "O Angeli supradicti, esto-te adjutores meae petitioni, &"</note>

Then let him call the Angels from the four parts of the world, who rule the Air on the same day in which he performs the work or experiment. These things being performed, let him read the Conjuration assigned for the day in which he makes the experiment, as we have spoken before; but if they shall be stubborn and rebellious, and will not yield themselves obedient, neither to the Conjuration assigned to the day, nor to the prayers made before, then use the Conjurations and Exorcisms following.

## An Exorcism of the Spirits of the Air.

We, made in the image of God, endowed with the power of God, and made by His will, through the most powerful and strengthened name of God *El* <note>original: "El" — a primary Hebrew name for God, meaning "Might" or "Power"</note>, strong and wonderful, we exorcise you (here he shall name the Spirits he would have appear, of what order soever they be) and command you by Him who spoke, and it was done, and by all the names of God, and by the name Adonay, El, Elohim, Elohe, Zebaoth, Elion, Escherchie, Jah, Tetragrammaton, Sadai, Lord God, most high, we exorcise you, and powerfully command you, that you appear immediately to us here beside the Circle in a fair form, namely human, and without any deformity or twisting. Come, all you of such kind, because we command you, by the name *Y* and *V* <note>Likely the Hebrew letters Yod and Vav</note> which Adam heard and spoke: and by the name of God *Agla*, which Lot heard, and was made safe with his family: and by the name *Joth*, which Jacob heard from the Angel wrestling with him, and was delivered from the hand of his brother Esau: and by the name *Anephexeton* <note>A mystical name of God often used in Solomonic magic</note>, which Aaron heard, and speaking, was made wise: and by the name *Zebaoth* <note>original: "Zebaoth" — Hebrew for "Of Hosts" or "Armies"</note>, which Moses named, and all the rivers and marshes of the land of Egypt [were turned...] <note>The text likely continues to describe the turning of waters into blood as found in Exodus</note>

and by these three secret names, *Agla, On, Tetragrammaton*, I adjure and summon you; and by these names, and by the other names of our Lord God Almighty, the living and the true, you who by your own fault were cast out of the Heavens even unto the place of hell, we exorcise and powerfully command you, by Him who spoke and it was done, whom all creatures obey; and by that terrible judgment of God; and by the sea, uncertain to all, like glass, which moves before the sight of the divine majesty, and is powerful; and by the four divine animals T. <note>The letter "T" likely indicates where the practitioner should make a sign of the cross or a specific ritual gesture</note> walking before the throne of divine majesty, having eyes in front and behind; and by the fire surrounding His throne; and by the holy Angels of the Heavens, T. and by her who is called the Church of God; and by the supreme wisdom of Almighty God, we powerfully exorcise you, that you appear here before us in front of the Circle, to perform our will in all things as it shall please us: by the throne of *Baldachia* <note>A mystical name for a celestial seat or authority</note>, and by this name *Primeumaton*, which *Moses* named, and *Dathan, Korah*, and *Abiram* <note>Biblical figures who rebelled against Moses and were swallowed by the earth</note> were plunged or swallowed into the depths of the abyss; and by the virtue of that name *Primeumaton*, with the whole host of Heaven compelling you, we curse you, we deprive you of every office, place, and joy of yours, even unto the depths of the abyss; and we place and banish you into eternal fire, and into the lake of fire and sulfur, until the final day of judgment, unless you appear here immediately before us, in front of the Circle, to do our will. In all things come, by these names, *Adonay Zebaoth* <note>original: "Adonay Zebaoth" — "Lord of Hosts"</note>, *Adonay Amioram*. Come, come, *Adonay* commands you.

<meta>catchword: nay</meta>

<summary>This page contains a powerful exorcism that uses biblical accounts of the Plagues of Egypt and the survival of prophets to command spirits of the air. It concludes with a severe threat, sentencing the spirits to the eternal lake of fire if they fail to appear before the practitioner's circle.</summary>

## A Prayer to God, to be said in the four parts of the world, in the Circle.

AMorule, Taneha, Latisten, Rabur, Taneha, Latisten, Escha,
Aladia, Alpha & Omega, Leyste, Oriston, Adonay: O my most
merciful heavenly Father, have mercy upon me, although a sin-
ner; make appear the arm of thy power in me this day (although
thy unworthy child) against these obstinate and pernicious Spirits,
that I by thy will may be made a contemplator of thy divine
works, and may be illustrated with all wisdom, and alwaies wor-
ship and glorifie thy name. I humbly implore and beseech thee,
that these Spirits which I call by thy judgement, may be bound
and constrained to come, and give true and perfect answers to those
things which I shall ask them, and that they may declare and shew
unto us those things which by me or us shall be commanded them,
not hurting any creature, neither injuring nor terrifying me or my
fellows, nor hurting any other creature, and affrighting no man; but
let them be obedient to my requests, in all these things which I com-
mand them. Then let him stand in the middle of the Circle,
and hold his hand towards the Pentacle <note>A magical talisman, often a disc of parchment or metal inscribed with protective symbols</note>, and say, *By the Pentacle of Solomon I have called [you], let them give me a true answer.* <note>original: "Per Pentaculum Salomonis advocavi, dent mihi responsum verum"</note>

Then let him say, *Beralanensis, Baldachiensis, Paumachia
and the seat of Apologia, by the Kings and great-hearted powers, and the
most powerful princes, by the spirit Liachide, and the ministers of the infernal seat* <note>original: "tartarea sedes" — referring to Tartarus or the underworld</note>: *Primac,*

> This prince of the seat of the Defense <note>original: "Apologia" — likely referring to a specific station or protective office</note> in the ninth cohort: I invoke you, and by invoking I conjure you, and being fortified by the power of the supreme Majesty, I powerfully command you, through Him who spoke and it was done, and whom all creatures obey: and by this ineffable name, <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>original: "יהוה Jehovah" — the four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH, considered the most potent name in Western magic</note>, in which the whole world was formed, at the sound of which the elements collapse, the air is shaken, the sea flows backward, the fire is extinguished, the earth trembles, and all the hosts of Celestial, Terrestrial, and Infernal beings tremble, are troubled, and fall down: so that quickly and without delay and with every excuse set aside, you may come from all parts of the world, and answer reasonably concerning all things whatsoever I shall ask, and that you come peacefully, visibly, and affably: now and without delay manifesting what we desire: being conjured by the name of the eternal, living, and true God *Helioren* <note>A divine name often used in medieval grimoires to compel spirits</note>, and fulfilling our commands, persisting always even unto the end, and to my intention, visible to us, and affable, with a clear voice, intelligible to us, and without any ambiguity.

---

## Visions and Apparitions.

> *When these things are rightly performed, there shall appear infinite visions and phantasms playing organs and all kinds of musical instruments; and this is done by the spirits so that, driven by terror, the companions might flee from the Circle, because they can do nothing against the Master. After this, you will see an infinite number of archers with an infinite multitude of horrible beasts: which so compose themselves as if they wished to devour the companions; and yet, let them fear nothing. Then the Priest or Master, applying his hand to the <term>Pentacle</term> <note>A consecrated disc or parchment inscribed with holy names, used as a shield and a badge of authority</note>, let him say:* Let your iniquity flee from here, by the power of the banner of God. *And then the Spirits are compelled to obey the master, and the companions shall see nothing more.*

Then let the <term>Exorcist</term> <note>In this context, the practitioner who "adjures" or commands spirits, rather than one who casts them out of a possessed person</note> say, stretching out his hand to the Pentacle, *Behold the Pentacle of Solomon, which I have brought before your presence: behold the person of the exorcist in the middle of the Exorcism, who is excellently fortified by God, intrepid, foresighted, and who, powerful in strength, has invoked and calls you by exorcising. Come therefore with haste in the power of these names, I Am that I Am, I Am that I Am, I Am that I Am <note>original: "Aye Saraye" — a transliteration of the Hebrew 'Ehyeh Asher Ehyeh' from Exodus 3:14</note>, do not delay to come, by the eternal names of the living and true God, My God <note>original: "Eloy"</note>, the Most High <note>original: "Archima"</note>, the Powerful <note>original: "Rabur"</note>: and by this present Pentacle, which above*

<summary>This page details the conjuration of spirits from the "ninth cohort" and describes the sensory illusions—such as music and terrifying beasts—that practitioners might experience. It provides instructions on using the Pentacle of Solomon to dispel these fears and command the spirits to appear in a clear, visible form.</summary>
