Gerard of Cremona, <note>Original: "Gerard Cremonenfis." A famous 12th-century scholar and translator known for bringing scientific and astrological works from Arabic and Greek into Latin.</note>

## Questions regarding the Second House.

If you wish to be informed concerning the substance <note>Meaning a person's material wealth or estate.</note> of any man—whether he shall be rich or not—look to the **Lord of the second** <term>Lord of the second</term> <note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign located at the beginning of the Second House in an astrological chart.</note>. If that planet is positioned with a **good Planet**, and there is also a **good Planet** within the second house itself, he shall be rich. But if the **Lord of the second** is joined with **evil Planets**, and an **evil Planet** is located in the second house, he shall be poor. <note>In traditional astrology, "good" planets (benefics) usually include Jupiter and Venus, while "evil" planets (malefics) include Saturn and Mars.</note>

If you wish to know whether you shall recover a lent item or not, look to see if there is an **evil Planet** in the second house that disagrees with its **Lord**; if so, the person holding the lent item will not willingly return it. But if there is a **good Planet** in the second house that agrees with its **Lord**, the item shall easily be recovered. If the **Lord of the second** is **exalted** <term>exalted</term> <note>Exaltation is a position in the zodiac where a planet is thought to be at its most powerful and dignified.</note> and is an **evil planet**, or if an **evil Planet** is with him in the second house, the person who keeps the deposited item will not willingly restore it, yet he shall be forced to do so whether he wishes to or not. And if an **evil Planet** is in the second house, it is still to be recovered. But if *Mercury* is in the second house such that he is its **Lord**, and he brings **contrariety** <note>Opposition or conflicting influences.</note>, then the item shall be recovered. If a **good Planet** is in the second house, he signifies recovery, even if he is the **Lord** of that house.

Observe, therefore, the concord and discord of the **Planets**: the *Moon* and *Jupiter* are friends, while the *Moon* and *Mars* are enemies; *Mercury* and the *Sun* are friends, while *Mercury* and *Venus* are enemies; *Venus* and *Jupiter* are friends, while *Jupiter* and the *Moon* are enemies. <note>The text here contains a contradiction common in period manuscripts, listing the Moon and Jupiter as both friends and enemies.</note>

The **Planets** are said to be friends when they agree in one nature and quality—such as *Mars* and the *Sun*, because both their natures are hot and dry—or when *Venus* and the *Moon* agree in coldness and moisture. They are also friends when they agree in substance and nature, as *Jupiter* and *Venus* are friends, or when the **House** of one is the **exaltation** of another, or vice versa.

<summary>This page provides astrological rules for determining a person's wealth and the likelihood of recovering lost or lent property based on the Second House. it also defines the relationships of "friendship" and "enmity" between planets based on their shared elemental qualities.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the practical application of astrology, moving from the Second House (wealth) to the Third House (siblings) and the Fourth House (home and property). The text maintains the instructional tone of a manual for a practicing astrologer or geomancer.</meta>

# Of Astronomical Geomancy.

## Questions of the Third House.

If you desire to know how many brethren <note>In this context, "brethren" refers to siblings in general, though the text distinguishes between brothers and sisters later.</note> a man has, look to the **Lord of the third** <term>Lord of the third</term> <note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign found at the start of the third house, which traditionally governs siblings and short journeys.</note>. It is held that the **Querent** <term>Querent</term> <note>The person asking the question of the astrologer.</note> has as many siblings as there are planets joined to that Lord. **Masculine Planets** signify brothers, and **female Planets** signify sisters.

Note that **Saturn**, **Mars**, the **Sun**, **Jupiter**, and the **Dragon's Head** <term>Dragon’s Head</term> <note>Also known as the North Node of the Moon, traditionally viewed as an increasing or beneficial point.</note> are masculine. The **Moon**, **Venus**, and the **Dragon's Tail** <term>Dragon’s Tail</term> <note>Also known as the South Node of the Moon, traditionally viewed as a decreasing or challenging point.</note> are feminine. However, **Mercury** is promiscuous <note>Meaning "neutral" or "changeable" in this context.</note>, being sometimes masculine and sometimes feminine: he is masculine when joined to masculine planets or in a masculine quarter of the **Zodiac**; he is feminine when joined to feminine planets or in a feminine quarter of the **Zodiac**.

## Questions of the Fourth House.

If you would know whether it be good for you to stay in any Land, City, Village, Territory, or House, or not, observe the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant</term> <note>The planet ruling the first house, representing the person themselves.</note>, as well as the Lords of the fourth and seventh houses. If the **Lord of the fourth** is in the **seventh house** and is well-placed, and the Lords of the first and tenth houses are also well-placed and with **good Planets**, then it is good for you to continue in your current place.

But if the **Lord of the seventh** is with a **good Planet**, and the **Lord of the fourth** is with an **evil Planet** <note>In traditional astrology, "good" planets are benefics (Jupiter, Venus) and "evil" planets are malefics (Saturn, Mars).</note>, then it is not good for you to abide there; for if you continue there, you shall suffer many losses and have evil reports <note>Defamation or a ruined reputation.</note> raised against you in that country.

If you would know when someone who is absent will return, observe the **Lord of the Ascendant**. If you find him in any of the four **Angles** <term>Angles</term> <note>The strongest houses in a chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th.</note>, he will return within that year. If he is not in an Angle, then see how far he is distant from the first Angle; he will stay away for as many years as there are houses between them.

If you wish to be informed of the dearth or plenty of things <note>Referring to economic conditions, such as crop yields or the cost of goods.</note>, observe the **strong Houses**, the **Succedents**, and the **Cadents**. <note>The houses are categorized by strength: Angles are strongest, Succedents (2, 5, 8, 11) are of middling strength, and Cadents (3, 6, 9, 12) are the weakest.</note>

# Gerard of Cremona

For the **strong Houses** <term>Angular Houses</term> <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which are the most powerful positions in an astrological chart.</note> signify dearth and scarcity, the **Succedents** <term>Succedent Houses</term> <note>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses, which follow the Angular houses and represent moderate strength.</note> a moderate season, neither too dear, nor too cheap; the **Cadents** <term>Cadent Houses</term> <note>The 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses, considered the weakest and signifying decline or abundance through lower prices.</note> signify plenty and profitableness of things. Consider also the Planets and their places; if they are in strong Houses, the things signified by those Planets will be rare. 

Note that **Saturn** signifies fields, vines, and tools for working in fields, leather, and of fruits: corn, acorns, **oak-apples** <note>Also known as gallnuts, these were used historically to produce high-quality ink.</note>, and pomegranates. **Jupiter** has oil, honey, silkworms, cloth, wine, and grass, and things that are **odoriferous** <note>Meaning fragrant or sweet-smelling.</note>. **Mars** signifies wine and flesh—especially hogs—wars, and armor, and such things as belong to them, and red garments. The **Sun** has signification of wheat and wine, purple colors, and cloth, and all things that are **assimilated** <note>Likened or related to.</note> unto gold, horses, and birds such as hawks and falcons. **Venus** signifies **fatness** <note>Likely referring to oils, lards, or rich foods.</note> and grapes, figs and dates, fish and pastimes. **Mercury** has barley, millet, grain, money, and quicksilver. The **Moon** signifies oats, milk, cheese, fire and salt, cows, rams, hens, and silver, and accordingly their plenty or scarcity.

If you would know whether a woman be with Child, or whether she will have any children or not, look if the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant</term> <note>The planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question.</note> be in the seventh, or the **Lord of the fifth** <term>Lord of the fifth</term> <note>The planet ruling the house of children and pleasure.</note> in the first, or the Lord of the first in the fifth, or if the Lord of the fifth be in the seventh, or if the Lord of the seventh be in the fifth, or the **Moon** with them; or if good Planets be in the first, or the fifth, or with the Lord of the fifth, or **Benefics** <note>The source uses a symbol "R", likely representing a benefic planet like Jupiter or the Sun in this context of fertility.</note> in **Angles** <term>Angles</term> <note>The four most powerful houses in the chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th).</note>, she is with child, or may have children.

But if you find none of them, but evil Planets in the same places, she neither is with child, neither will she have any children. And if there be both good and evil Planets in the said places, then happily she may have children, but they will not live. But if **Cancer**, **Scorpio**, or **Pisces** <note>These are the three "Water signs," traditionally associated with fertility and abundance.</note> shall be in the first or fifth House, she may have children; but if **Leo** and **Virgo** <note>Traditionally known as "Barren signs" in medieval astrology.</note> be there, she is not with child, neither

If you desire to know whether rumors be true or false, see if you find ***Saturn***, ***Mars***, or the ***Dragon's Tail*** <term>Dragon's Tail</term> <note>The South Node of the Moon, usually considered an unfavorable influence in medieval astrology.</note> in the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant</term> <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question.</note>; because if they be so, then the rumors are false. Likewise, if ***Mercury*** be in the first, the news is false: but if the ***Moon*** be in the first in a feminine sign, or joined with the Lord of the Ascendant in a feminine sign, then the rumors are true; also if good planets be in the first, fifth, or ninth, and feminine signs, they are true; but if otherwise, they are not.

If you would know whether anyone that is absent will return, and when; see the Lord of the Ascendant and the first house, which if you find them together, for certain he will come, and is now beginning his journey. Likewise, if the **Lord of the fifth** be in the first, or with the Lord of the first—and if he be **in his fall** <term>in his fall</term> <note>A position in the zodiac where a planet is at its weakest.</note>—the messenger is sick on his way; but if the Lord of the fifth be **exalted** <term>exalted</term> <note>A position where a planet’s influence is at its highest potential.</note>, then he comes joyfully. And if he be in a **cadent sign** <term>cadent sign</term> <note>Signs associated with the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses, often indicating a lack of strength or a state of "falling away."</note>, he shall be grievously afflicted with sickness, or shall die.

If you would know if he brings that with him for which he went, or not, behold the **Lord of the seventh** <term>Lord of the seventh</term> <note>The planet ruling the house of partnerships and "the other person" involved in the quest.</note>; which if he be good, he brings that which he sought <note>OCR reads "fought," but the context of a mission suggests "sought."</note> for; and if he be in his fall, or an evil planet be there, he brings nothing with him.

### Of Astronomical Geomancy.

...the **Lord** <term>The planet that rules the sign on a house's cusp</term> of the **sixth House** <note>The house associated with servants, employees, and small animals.</note>; which if he be in the **second House** <note>The house of personal wealth and possessions.</note>, or with the Lord of the second, there were many thieves; and if they be in the **third House** <note>The house of siblings, neighbors, and short journeys.</note>, the brethren or kinsmen of the **Querent** <term>The person who asks the question of the astrologer</term> have committed the theft.

If you would know whether the thief still remains in the Town: if they be in **succedent Houses** <term>Houses 2, 5, 8, and 11, which follow the powerful 'angle' houses</term>, he is not gone far off; but if they be in **cadent Houses** <term>Houses 3, 6, 9, and 12, which 'fall away' from the angles and indicate weakness or distance</term>, he is far remote.

If you desire to know towards what Country the thief is fled, see in what **Sign** <term>Zodiac sign</term> the Lord of the **seventh House** <note>In this context, the house representing the thief.</note> is; for if he be in **Aries** <term>The Ram</term>, he is in the middle of the East part. If in **Taurus** <term>The Bull</term>, in the South towards the East. If in **Gemini** <term>The Twins</term>, in the West towards the South. If in **Cancer** <term>The Crab</term>, full North. If in **Leo** <term>The Lion</term>, in the East towards the North. If in **Virgo** <term>The Maiden</term>, in the South towards the West. If in **Libra** <term>The Scales</term>, full West. If in **Scorpio** <term>The Scorpion</term>, in the North near the West. If in **Sagittary** <term>The Archer</term>, in the East near the North. If in **Capricorn** <term>The Goat</term>, full South. If in **Aquary** <term>The Water-Bearer</term>, in the West towards the North. And if in **Pisces** <term>The Fish</term>, in the North towards the West.

If you would know whether the thief has carried all the stolen things away with him, see the Lord of the seventh and the **eighth House** <note>The house of other people's money and debts.</note>; and if the Lord of the seventh be in an **Angle** <term>Houses 1, 4, 7, or 10, representing strength and the present moment</term>, he had a desire to carry away the same with him, but could not. If the Lord of the eighth be in a **mean House** <note>Also known as a succedent house.</note>, or in a cadent House, and the Lord of the second in a strong House, he has carried the theft wholly with him. And if the Lord of the seventh and the eighth be both in cadent Houses, he neither carried it away, nor has it. See by the seventh who is his companion, and what is his gain.

If you would know the descent or **nobility** <note>Social status and lineage were primary concerns for early modern clients of astrology.</note> of a man or woman, look unto the Lord of the seventh; which if you find him in Angles, and the Lord of the **first House** <note>The house representing the Querent or the person being compared.</note> in Succedents or cadents, the woman is more noble than the man. But if the Lord of the **Ascendant** <term>The sign rising on the Eastern horizon; the Lord of the first house</term> be in an Angle, and the Lord of the seventh in a succedent or cadent House, the man is more noble than the woman. And after the same manner you may judge of two companions, or of any other persons whatsoever. And if the Lord of the seventh be in the **ninth House** <note>The house of long journeys, religion, and foreign lands.</note>, he will take a wife out of a foreign Country.

### Of Astronomical Geomancy <note>Though titled "Geomancy" (divination by earth), the text describes "Horary Astrology," which interprets a chart cast for the specific moment a question is asked.</note>

*War*, see the seventh and the first, and their *Lords* <term>The specific planets that rule the zodiac signs located at the start of those houses</term>: for the first *House* and his *Lord* signifies the *Querent* <term>The person asking the question</term>; and the seventh *House* and his *Lord* the *adversary*. In astrology, a planet is retrograde when it appears to move backward, often signifying a loss of power or a reversal of fortune.</note> in the seventh, the *Querent* shall be overcome, or taken, or slain. And if the *Lords* of them both be in the first, and there be good *Planets* from the part of the first *House*, unto the end of the *House* which is the middle of the *Question*; and if evil *Planets* do possess the other half of the *Question*, that is to say, from the seventh unto the end of the twelfth house, the *adversary* shall overcome. 

But if both the *Lords* shall be in the *Ascendant* <term>The First House, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, and if they be good from the part of the first, and evil from the part of the seventh, they shall both suffer great loss; but the *Querent* shall have the better in the end. And if the *Lord* of the seventh be in the second, or with his *Lord*; or the *Lord* of the second in the seventh, or with the *Lord* of the seventh, it signifies the death of the enemy <note>In "turned houses," the 8th house of the adversary is the 2nd house of the chart (seven houses plus eight houses). Therefore, the 2nd house predicts the opponent's death.</note>.

If you would know whether *War* shall continue long or not, if mean or meanly <note>Meaning of moderate or average duration.</note>; if the *Lords* of the first and the seventh do agree, the parties shall be pacified after the *War*.

...the *Lords* <term>the ruling planets of specific sections of the sky</term> of the first and the second *houses*, for those places to which you would go, the place where you are now, and the profit which you might gain there; and the seventh and the eighth *houses*, and their *Lords*, for the place to which you would go, and the gain which you may get there. And if the *Lord* of the *Ascendant* and the *Moon* be separated from the *Fortunes* <term>the lucky planets, Jupiter and Venus</term>, and joined to evil *Planets*, then it is not good for you to move yourself, nor to do your business. Or thus: see the *Moon*; and if the *Planet* from which she is separated be better than that to which she is joined, do not move: and if the *Planet* which she is joined to be better than that from which she is separated, then go.

### *Questions regarding the Eighth House.*

Concerning any man or woman, if you would know what kind of death they shall die, see if *Leo*, *Scorpio*, or <insert>Retrograde</insert> <note>original: "†R.†", an abbreviation for Retrograde, meaning a planet appearing to move backward in the sky.</note> *Mars* be in the eighth *house*; if so, the party shall die by a beast. And if *Saturn* be in the eighth, or with the *Lord* of the eighth in *Leo*, *Scorpio*, *Cancer*, or *Pisces*, he shall die in water. And if an evil *Planet* be in the eighth, or with the *Lord* of the eighth; or if *Mars* or the *Dragon’s Tail* <term>the South Node of the Moon, traditionally viewed as a point of depletion or misfortune</term> be there, he shall die by fire, iron, or of a fever. And if there be a good *Planet* in the eighth, or with the Lord of the eighth, he shall die a good <note>meaning a natural or peaceful</note> death.

## Questions of the ninth House.

Regarding long journeys, observe whether the **Lord** <term>the planet that rules a specific zodiac sign</term> of the eighth house is accompanied by favorable Planets. If **Saturn** is in the ninth house and *exalted* <term>positioned in a sign where its influence is strongest</term> in the tenth house, but is not in his own House <note>the zodiac sign he naturally rules, such as Capricorn or Aquarius</note>, do not go: for you will meet with many obstacles and War. And if an evil Planet <note>Historically called "malefics," usually Saturn or Mars</note> is in the ninth house, or with the **Lord** of the ninth, and the **Lord** of the ninth is in his *fall* <term>the sign where a planet's influence is at its weakest</term>, the traveler shall suffer great damage on the way. For if he goes by water, he shall suffer shipwreck; and if by Land, he shall have misfortunes, be captured, or die. If **Saturn** is in the ninth, or with its **Lord**, go securely <note>Though usually seen as a bringer of hardship, Saturn here may imply a slow but stable journey</note>. And if a good Planet <note>Historically called "benefics," usually Jupiter or Venus</note> is in the ninth, or with the **Lord** of the ninth, the way is good and secure. And if **Mars** is in the ninth, you must not go: for you will meet with mortal enemies on the way. And if the **Lord** of the ninth is with an evil † Planet <note>The dagger symbol in the text likely denotes a harmful or "malefic" planet</note>, or the **Sun** <note>The Sun can be "malefic" in this context if it is so close to another planet that it "burns" its influence, a state called combustion</note>, it signifies ill: but he shall not be captured. And if the **Lord** of the ninth has a good Planet near him, he shall escape: but if the planet is evil, he shall be captured. If **Venus** is in the ninth, or with the **Lord** of the ninth, the journey will be good, because he shall have comfort from women. And if **Mercury** is in the ninth, and the **Lord** of the ninth is with good Planets, the way will be very good: and if he is with evil Planets, it will be bad. And the same is said of the **Moon**, as was said of **Mercury**. If the **Dragon's Tail** <term>the South Node of the Moon, traditionally associated with loss or "draining" energy</term> is in the ninth, he will meet with thieves, or some evil people. And if the **Dragon's Head** <term>the North Node of the Moon, associated with growth and favor</term> is in the ninth, the way will be good, because he shall be accompanied by Noblemen. And in this manner you may judge the third House <note>The house that governs short-distance travel, such as trips to neighboring towns</note> concerning short journeys.

If you would know when the journey shall be accomplished, look to the **Lord** of the ninth, and judge according to his strength or weakness; because depending on the place where he is, he indicates years, months, or days. And so you shall judge concerning his stay, and about what time he will

And this I say concerning his return.

If you would know whether he shall return from his journey with an imperfect voyage <note>A journey that is cut short, abandoned, or fails to achieve its primary goal</note> or not, see if the **Moon** is joined with the **Lord** of the first, the third, or the ninth house, and the Planet thereof be in its *fall* <term>the specific zodiac sign where a planet's influence is at its weakest</term>; because if it be so, he shall return with an imperfect voyage. And if the **Moon** be in her *exaltation* <term>the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is most harmoniously strengthened</term>, the journey shall be completed quickly. And if there be two strong Planets and one *cadent* <term>positioned in the 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th houses, which were traditionally seen as "falling away" from the powerful angles and thus weaker</term>, the journey shall be made; and if one be strong and another in his *fall*, he shall turn back.

### Questions of the tenth House.

If you wish to know whether you shall have any honor or benefit from a King, Bishop, or Lord <note>These figures represent the high-ranking authorities and paternal figures traditionally governed by the tenth house</note> or not, look to the first House and the ninth House, and their **Lords**: and if the **Lord** of the first be in the ninth, or with the **Lord** of the ninth, or with any other favorable Planet; or if the **Lord** of the ninth be in the first, or with the **Lord** of the first, or with any other favorable Planet, such as *Venus*, *Jupiter*, or the **Dragons Head** <term>Also known as the North Node, a point in the sky associated with increase, growth, and good fortune</term>; or if any of them be the **Lord** of the ninth, or the Ruler <note>The original text uses the abbreviation "R." for Ruler or *Rector*</note> of the first, he shall receive honor and benefits from them.

And if you would know whether he shall have it in his own Country or in a foreign Country, look if the **Lord** of the ninth be in *angles* <term>The 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, which indicate local or immediate action</term>; then it shall be in his own Country. And if in *succedents* <term>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses, which follow the angles and suggest things nearby but not immediate</term>, it shall be nearby; but if in *cadent* Houses, it shall be very far away.

### Questions of the eleventh House.

If you wish to know when it is favorable to launch a ship to sea, look to the **Ascendant** <term>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question</term>. If the sign is *stable* <term>a "fixed" sign like Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius, which represents endurance</term>, the ship will be heavy and slow; but if the **Lord** <term>the planet that rules the ascending sign</term> is positioned with a good planet, she will be able to carry a great weight. If the **Ascendant** is *unstable* <term>a "cardinal" or "mutable" sign, representing movement</term> and paired with a good planet, the ship will be fast and carry a good cargo. If the **Ascendant** is *mean* <note>average or moderate</note>, the ship's qualities will be moderate. In this same way, you may also judge matters concerning a horse, if a question is asked about one.

If any *unstable* sign is ascending, and its **Lord** is in its *exaltation* <term>the sign where a planet’s influence is most powerfully and harmoniously expressed</term> or otherwise fortunate, and the **Moon** regards him with a harsh aspect <note>original: "lowring," referring to a difficult astrological alignment like a square or opposition</note> or a *Sextile* <term>a harmonious 60-degree angle between planets</term>, then let the ship be set to the water, for she will be very swift. If any evil is predicted for her, or it seems she might be drawn into trouble, then launch her when a *stable* sign is ascending, or when the **Moon** is in the third, fifth, eighth, ninth, or tenth house or mansion.

If you would know what kind of wind the ship shall encounter, observe the **Ascendant** and its **Lord**, noting whether he is with good or evil planets and in what place, and judge accordingly.

If you desire a strong wind, spread your sails at the rising of **Aquarius**; if you want a light wind, spread your canvas when **Libra** is ascending; if you want a moderate wind, then set your sails under **Gemini** <note>These three signs—Aquarius, Libra, and Gemini—comprise the "Air Triplicity," which is why they are used here to control the winds</note>.

### Questions of the twelfth House.

Regarding imprisonment, consider the twelfth and the first houses; if the **Lord** of the twelfth is in the first, or positioned with the **Lord** of the first, etc. <note>The twelfth house traditionally governs "self-undoing," secrets, and places of confinement like prisons</note>.

### Arbatel <note>original Hebrew: ארבעתאל. The name is often interpreted as "The Fourfold God" or "The Altar of God."</note>

# Of the MAGIC of the Ancients,
## The Greatest Study of Wisdom.

> *In all things, ask counsel of the Lord; and do not think, speak, or do anything wherein God is not your counselor.*

->Proverbs 11<-

> *He who walks deceitfully reveals secrets; but he who is of a faithful spirit conceals the matter.*

---

# ARBATEL of MAGIC:
->OR,<-
## The Spiritual Wisdom of the Ancients,
Including the Wise-men of the people of God and the **Magi** <term>Magi: Ancient priests or wise men, particularly from the East, credited with deep knowledge of astrology and the divine</term> of the Gentiles: for the illustration of the glory of God and His love for Mankind.

Now, for the first time, brought out of darkness into the light, against all **Caco-magicians** <term>Caco-magicians: From the Greek "kakos" (evil); those who practice harmful or "black" magic</term> and those who despise the gifts of God; for the benefit and delight of all those who truly and piously love the creatures of God, and use them with thanksgiving to the honor of God and the profit of themselves and their neighbors.

<meta>catchword: Con-</meta>

<summary>This is the title page for the "Arbatel of Magic," a famous 16th-century grimoire that frames the practice of magic as a spiritual and pious pursuit. It distinguishes "true" magic from "caco-magic" (evil magic) and emphasizes the need for divine guidance.</summary>

<keywords>Arbatel, spiritual wisdom, Magi, caco-magicians, title page, divine magic, Proverbs 11, theurgy</keywords>

--- Page 194 ---
<meta>This page continues from the title page (page 177), which established the spiritual and pious intent of the Arbatel. Here, the text outlines the structure of the work, dividing the study of magic into nine distinct books or volumes.</meta>

<page-num>178</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

->Containing nine Volumes, and seven groups of seven<- <note>original: "septenaries," referring to a set of seven things, in this case, sets of seven aphorisms.</note>
->APHORISMS.<-

The first is called *Isagoge*, or, A Book of the Institutions of Magic: or, Spiritual Wisdom <note>original Greek: πνευματικής (pneumatikēs)</note>; which in forty-nine Aphorisms comprehends the most general Precepts of the whole Art.

The second is Microcosmical Magic, concerning what the Microcosm <term>Microcosm: "The little world," referring to Man as a miniature reflection of the entire Universe or Macrocosm</term> has effected Magically, by his Spirit and the Genius assigned to him from his Birth—that is, spiritual wisdom—and how the same is performed.

The third is Olympic Magic, describing the manner in which a man may act and be acted upon by the spirits of *Olympus* <note>In this context, Olympic spirits refer to the seven planetary spirits who govern the world, rather than the Greek gods.</note>.

The fourth is Hesiodiacal and Homerical Magic, which teaches the operations performed by the Spirits called *Cacodæmones* <note>original Greek: κακοδαίμονες (kakodaimones). While often meaning "evil spirits," the text specifies these are "not adversaries to mankind," suggesting they are nature spirits or spirits of the air.</note>.

The fifth is Roman or Sibylline Magic, which acts and operates with Guardian Spirits and Lords to whom the various parts of the earth are distributed. This is highly distinguished Magic <note>original Latin: valde insignis Magia</note>. To this also the doctrine of the Druids is referred.

The sixth is Pythagorean Magic, which only acts with Spirits to whom is given the teaching of the Arts, such as Medicine <note>original: "Physick"</note>, Mathematics, Alchemy, and similar types of Arts.

The seventh is the Magic of *Apollonius*, and the like, and agrees with Roman and Microcosmical Magic; it has only this unique feature: it has power over the spirits that are hostile to mankind.

The eighth is Hermetic—that is, Egyptian Magic—and differs not much from Divine Magic.

The ninth is that wisdom which depends solely upon the Word of God; and this is called Prophetic Magic.

<meta>catchword: The</meta>

<summary>This page provides an overview of the nine "Tomes" or books that comprise the Arbatel, categorizing different types of magic ranging from the planetary and national to the scientific and prophetic.</summary>

# The first Volume of the Book of
# Arbatel of Magic,

## THE INTRODUCTION <note>original Greek: *Isagoge*, a term used for an introductory manual or treatise.</note>.

> In the Name of the Creator of all things both visible and invisible, who reveals his Mysteries out of his Treasures to those who call upon him; and fatherly and mercifully bestows those his Secrets upon us without measure. May he grant to us, through his only-begotten Son Jesus Christ our Lord, his ministering spirits, the revealers of his secrets, that we may write this Book of *Arbatel*, concerning the greatest Secrets which are lawful for man to know, and to use them without offending God. ***Amen.***

## The first group of seven Aphorisms <note>original: "Septenary." The Arbatel is famously organized into groups of seven short, pithy statements or "aphorisms."</note>.
### *The first Aphorism.*

Whoever would know Secrets, let him know how to keep secret things secretly; and to reveal those things that are to be revealed, and to seal those things which are to be sealed: and *not to give holy things to dogs, nor cast pearls before swine.* <note>This is a quote from the Bible (Matthew 7:6), warning the reader that sacred or "magical" knowledge must be protected from those who cannot appreciate its value or who might misuse it.</note>

### *Aphorism 2.*

In all things, call upon the Name of the Lord: and without prayer unto God through his only-begotten Son, do not thou undertake to do or think anything. And use the Spirits given and attributed unto thee, as Ministers, without rashness and presumption, as the messengers of God; having a due reverence towards the Lord of Spirits. And the remainder of thy life do thou accomplish, demeaning thyself peaceably, to the honor of God, and the profit of thyself and thy neighbor.

### *Aphorism 3.*

Live to thyself, and the Muses <note>The Muses were the nine Greek goddesses of inspiration in the arts and sciences; here, the text suggests a life dedicated to study and creative contemplation.</note>: avoid the friendship of the Multitude: be thou covetous <note>In this context, "covetous" means to be extremely careful and stingy with one's time, not wasting it on trivialities.</note> of time, beneficial to all men. ### *Aphorism 4.*

Resist the temptations of the Tempter <note>The "Tempter" is a traditional title for the Devil.</note>, by the Word of God. Flee from earthly things; seek after heavenly things. ### Aphorism 5.

Thou shalt love the Lord thy God with all thy heart, and with *all thy strength, and thy neighbour as thy self* : And the Lord will keep thee as the apple of his eye, <note>A biblical metaphor for something that is cherished and protected with extreme care.</note> and will deliver thee from all evil, and will replenish thee with all good ; and nothing shall thy soul desire, but thou shalt be fully endued therewith, so that it be contingent to the salvation of thy soul and body.

### Aphorism 6.

### Aphorism 7.

For there is no power either in heaven or on earth, or hell, which does not descend from God; and without his permission, they can neither give nor bring forth into any action anything they have.

### Aphorism 9.

That is the highest wisdom which is from God; and next, that which is in spiritual creatures; afterwards, in physical creatures; fourthly, in Nature and natural things. The *Spirits* that are fallen <note>original: "apostate"</note> and reserved for the final judgment follow these after a long interval. Sixthly, the ministers of punishments in hell, and those who are obedient to God. Seventhly, the *Pigmies* <note>In early modern occultism, "Pigmies" refers to elemental spirits of the earth, later commonly known as gnomes.</note> do not occupy the lowest place, nor do those who inhabit the elements and elementary things. It is appropriate, therefore, to know and distinguish all the differences between the wisdom of the Creator and the Creatures, so that it may be clearly revealed to us what we ought to take for our use from everything, and that we may know in truth how and in what manner that may be done. For truly every creature is ordained for some profitable end for human nature and for its service, as the *Holy Scriptures*, *Reason*, and *Experience* testify.

---

### Aphorism 10.

*God the Father Almighty*, Creator of heaven and earth, and of all things visible and invisible, proposes in the *Holy Scriptures* that he keeps an eye over us; and as a tender father who loves his children, he teaches us what is profitable and what is not; what we are to avoid and what we are to embrace: then he draws us to obedience with great <meta>catchword: promises</meta>

<summary>This page explains that magical names and symbols derive power solely from God's appointment rather than their form, and establishes a seven-fold hierarchy of wisdom ranging from the divine to elemental spirits like Pigmies.</summary>

promises of physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> and eternal benefits, and deters us (with the threat of punishments) from those things which are not profitable for us. Therefore, leaf through those holy writings both night and day with your own hand, so that you may be happy in present things, and blessed for all eternity. Do this, as the holy Books have taught you, and you shall live.

## Aphorism 11.

The number **Four** is Pythagorean <note>Referring to the teachings of Pythagoras, who held that the number four—the Tetrad—represented perfection and the soul.</note> and the first square <note>original: "Quadrate"</note>; therefore, let us place here the foundation of all wisdom, after the wisdom of **God** revealed in the holy **Scriptures**, and the **Considerations** proposed in **Nature**.

Therefore, appoint to him who solely depends upon **God**, the wisdom of every creature to serve and obey him, **willing or unwilling** <note>original: "nolens volens"</note>. And in this, the **Omnipotence of God** shines forth. It consists, therefore, in this: that we discern the creatures which serve us from those that are unwilling; and that we may learn how to adapt the wisdom and offices of every creature to ourselves. This Art is not granted except by divine means. To whom **God** wills, he reveals his secrets; but to the person to whom he will not bestow anything out of his treasuries, that person shall attain to nothing without the will of **God**.

Therefore we ought truly to desire <term>spiritual knowledge <note>original: "τὴν πνευματικὴν ἐπιστήμην"</note></term> from **God** alone, who will mercifully impart these things to us. For he who has given us his Son, and commanded us to pray for his holy Spirit, how much more will he subject the whole of creation to us, both things visible and invisible? Whatsoever you ask, you shall receive. Beware that you do not abuse the gifts of **God**, and all things shall work together for your salvation. And before all things, be watchful in this: That your names be written in heaven; this is of greater weight than the spirits being obedient to you, as **Christ** admonishes. <note>A reference to Luke 10:20, where Jesus tells his disciples to rejoice in their salvation rather than their power over demons.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the instruction to study scripture for eternal life and introduces Aphorism 11, which explains how spiritual wisdom and the command over creatures are gifts from God, emphasizing the Pythagorean significance of the number four.</summary>
<keywords>Aphorism 11, Pythagoras, Quadrate, Holy Scripture, spiritual knowledge, divine secrets, divine will, obedience of spirits, salvation</keywords>

### Aphorism 12.

In the *Acts* of the *Apostles*, the Spirit says unto *Peter* after the Vision, *Go down, and doubt not but I have sent them*, when he was sent for from *Cornelius* the Centurion. After this manner, in vocal words, are all disciplines delivered by the holy Angels of God, as it appears out of the Monuments <note>Likely referring to the ancient records or architectural inscriptions of Egypt.</note> of the Egyptians. And these things afterwards were vitiated and corrupted with human opinions; and by the instigation of evil spirits, who sow tares <note>A biblical metaphor for weeds or destructive elements sown among good crops.</note> amongst the children of disobedience, as it is manifest out of *St. Paul*, and *Hermes Trismegistus* <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure considered the author of the Hermetic Corpus, a series of texts on philosophy, magic, and alchemy.</note>. There is no other manner of restoring these Arts, than by the doctrine of the holy Spirits of God; because true faith comes by hearing. But because you may be certain of the truth, and may not doubt whether the spirits that speak with you do declare things true or false, let it only depend upon your faith in God; that you may say with *Paul*, *I know on whom I trust*. If no sparrow can fall to the ground without the will of the Father which is in heaven, how much more will not God suffer you to be deceived, O you of little faith, if you depend wholly upon God, and adhere only to him?

### Aphorism 13.

The Lord lives; and all things which live, do live in him. And he is truly **Yahweh** <note>original Hebrew: יהוה; the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of the God of Israel.</note>, who has given unto all things that they be that which they are: and by his word alone, through his Son, has produced all things out of nothing, which are in being. He calls all the stars, and all the host of heaven by their names. He therefore knows the true strength and nature of things, the order and policy <note>The organized governance or internal structure.</note> of every creature visible and invisible, to whom God has revealed the names of his creatures. It remains also, that he receive power from God, to extract the virtues in nature, and hidden secrets of the creature; and to produce their

Your goal, therefore, should be to obtain the names of the Spirits—that is, to understand their powers and their specific duties—and to know how they are placed and appointed by God to serve you. This is just as *Raphael* <note>An archangel whose name means "God heals"; he appears in the Book of Tobit.</note> was sent to *Tobias* so that he might heal his father, protect his son from dangers, and lead him to a wife. In the same way, *Michael* <note>An archangel and leader of the heavenly host; his name means "Who is like God?"</note>, the strength of God, governs the people of God; and *Gabriel* <note>The archangel of revelation and messenger of divine announcements.</note>, the messenger of God, was sent to *Daniel*, *Mary*, and *Zachary*, the father of *John the Baptist*. A spirit shall be given to you who desire him, and he will teach you whatever your soul desires regarding the nature of things. You must use his service with trembling and fear of your Creator, Redeemer, and Sanctifier—that is to say, the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit. Do not let any opportunity for learning slip away, and be watchful in your calling, and you will lack nothing that is necessary for you.

### Aphorism 14.

Your soul lives forever through Him who created you; therefore, call upon the Lord your God, and Him only shall you serve. You will do this if you fulfill the purpose for which you were ordained by God, and perform what you owe to both God and your neighbor. God requires a mind that honors His Son and keeps the words of His Son in your heart; if you honor Him, you have done the will of your Father who is in heaven. To your neighbor, you owe acts of kindness <note>original: "offices of humanity"</note>, and you should strive to lead all people who come to you to honor the Son. This is the essence of the Law and the Prophets <note>A reference to Matthew 22:40, summarizing the core ethical requirements of the Bible.</note>. In worldly matters, you should call upon God as a father, asking that He would give you all the necessities of this life; and you should help your neighbor with the gifts God bestows upon you, whether those gifts are spiritual or physical.

> *Because I earnestly desire to perfectly know the arts of this life, and those things which are necessary for us—which are so overwhelmed in darkness and polluted with infinite human opinions that I, by my own power, can attain no knowledge in them unless you teach me: Grant me, therefore, one of your spirits, who may teach me those things which you would have me to know and learn, to your praise and glory, and for the benefit of our neighbor. Give me also an apt and teachable heart, that I may easily understand those things which you shall teach me, and may hide them in my understanding, so that I may bring them forth as if from your inexhaustible treasures, for all necessary uses. And give me grace, that I may use such gifts of yours humbly, with fear and trembling, through our Lord Jesus Christ, with your holy Spirit. Amen.*

## The third Septenary <note>A "Septenary" is a group or sequence of seven. The Arbatel is organized into seven groups of seven aphorisms each.</note>.

->Aphorism 15.<-

They are called **Olympic spirits** <note>In the context of this book, these are seven powerful spirits who govern the world under God’s authority. Each corresponds to one of the seven classical planets: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, the Sun, Venus, Mercury, and the Moon.</note>, who inhabit the **firmament** <note>The sphere of the heavens or the sky where the stars are fixed.</note> and the stars of the firmament: and the office of these spirits is to declare **Destinies**, and to administer **charms of fate** <note>Original: "fatal Charms." These are influences or magical operations related to a person’s destiny or the natural order of the world, rather than "deadly" charms.</note>, as far as God pleases to permit them. For nothing—neither an evil spirit nor an evil Destiny—shall be able to hurt him who has the Most High for his refuge. If, therefore, any of the Olympic spirits shall teach or declare that which the star to which he is appointed portends, he can nevertheless bring nothing into action unless he is permitted by the Divine power. It is God alone who gives them the power to carry it out. To God, the maker of all things,

### Aphorism 16.

There are seven different governments of the Spirits of **Olympus** <note>In the context of this book, "Olympus" refers to the celestial firmament or the spiritual heavens rather than the physical mountain in Greece.</note>, through whom God has appointed the entire frame and universe of this world to be governed. Their visible stars <note>In traditional astrology and magic, these spirits are associated with the seven "planets" known to the ancient world: Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, the Sun, Venus, Mercury, and the Moon.</note> are **ARATRON**, **BETHOR**, **PHALEG**, **OCH**, **HAGITH**, **OPHIEL**, and **PHUL**, according to the Olympic speech <note>The specific naming system or language used by these celestial beings.</note>. Every one of these has under him a mighty **host** <note>original: "Militia"</note> in the firmament.

**ARATRON** rules 49 visible Provinces.
**BETHOR** rules 32.
**PHALEG** rules 35.
**OCH** rules 28.
**HAGITH** rules 21.
**OPHIEL** rules 14.
**PHUL** rules 7.

So there are 186 Olympic Provinces in the whole Universe, in which these seven Governors exercise their power. All of these are elegantly described in the study of **Astronomy**. However, in this place, we must explain the manner in which these Princes and Powers may be drawn into communication. **Aratron** appears in the first hour of **Saturday**, and gives very true answers concerning his provinces and those who dwell within them. In the same way, the others appear in order on their own days and hours. Furthermore, each one of them rules for a period of 490 years. The beginning of their <term>simple anomaly</term> <note>A term from traditional astronomy referring to a planet's position in its cycle; here it indicates the start of a spirit's chronological reign.</note>, occurring in the 60th year before the Birth of Christ, marked the start of the administration of **Bethor**; it lasted until the year of our Lord 430. He was succeeded by **Phaleg**, who ruled until the year 920. Then...

### Aphorism 17.

Magically the *Princes* of the seven *Governors* <note>The seven primary spirits who rule the celestial spheres.</note> are called simply, in that time, day, and hour wherein they rule visibly or invisibly, by their Names and Offices which God hath given unto them; and by proposing <note>In this context, "proposing" means presenting or drawing the symbol during a ritual.</note> their Character <note>A "character" is a magical seal or sigil unique to a specific spirit.</note> which they have given or confirmed.

The Governor *Aratron* hath in his power those things which he doth naturally, that is, after the same manner and subject as those things which in Astronomy <note>In the 16th and 17th centuries, "astronomy" often encompassed what we now call astrology—the study of planetary influences on human affairs.</note> are ascribed to the power of *Saturn*.

->*Those things which he doth of his own free will, are,*<-

1. That he can convert any thing into a stone in a moment, either animal or plant, retaining the same object to the sight <note>This refers to petrifaction—turning living things to stone while maintaining their original shape.</note>.
2. He converteth treasures into coals, and coals into treasure.
3. He giveth familiars <note>Minor spirits or "household" entities that assist the practitioner.</note> with a definite power.
4. He teacheth *Alchemy, Magic,* and *Medicine* <term>Physick: The historical term for the practice of medicine and the study of natural philosophy.</term>.
5. He reconcileth the subterranean spirits <note>Spirits of the earth, often associated with gnomes or guardians of mines.</note> to men; maketh hairy men <note>Likely a reference to the "Wild Men" or *pilosi* of folklore, sometimes considered nature spirits or guardians.</note>.
6. He causeth one to be invisible.
7. The barren he maketh fruitful, and giveth long life.

### *The most general Precepts of this Secret.*

1. Every **Governor** <note>Refers to the seven Olympic Spirits who rule the various regions of the world and the celestial spheres.</note> acts with all his spirits in two ways: either naturally—meaning always in the same consistent manner—or by their own free will, provided God does not prevent them.
2. Every Governor is able to perform all things that occur naturally over a long period of time using prepared materials; however, they can also perform these things instantly using materials that have not been prepared. For example, **Och** <note>The Olympic Spirit associated with the Sun.</note>, the Prince of Solar things, produces gold in the mountains over a long time; he does it in less time through the **Chemical Art** <note>Alchemy; the forerunner of modern chemistry which sought to refine and perfect materials.</note>; and Magically, he does it in a single moment.
3. The true and divine Magician may use all of God's creatures and the powers of the Governors of the world at his own will, because the Governors of the world are obedient to such a magician. They come when they are called and carry out

...execute <note>The text begins mid-word, completing "execute" from the previous page.</note> their commands: but God is the Author thereof: as **Joshua** <note>A biblical leader; the text refers to the Book of Joshua 10:12–13, where God allows Joshua to command the sun and moon to stand still.</note> caused the Sun to stand still in heaven.

They send some of their Spirits to the mean <note>Common or lower-ranking.</note> Magicians, which do obey them only in some determinate business: but they hear not the false Magicians, but expose them to the deceits of the devils, and cast them into divers dangers, by the command of God; as the Prophet **Jeremiah** testifieth, in his eighth Chapter, concerning the **Jews**. <note>Referring to Jeremiah 8, which discusses the punishment of those who turned to false gods and celestial worship.</note>

4. In all the elements there are the seven Governors with their hosts, who do move with the equal motion of the firmament; and the inferiors do always depend upon the superiors, as it is taught in Philosophy. <note>This reflects the Hermetic principle of "as above, so below," suggesting that earthly elements are governed by celestial movements.</note>

5. A man that is a true Magician, is brought forth a Magician from his mother's womb: others, who do give themselves to this office, are unhappy. This is that which **John the Baptist** speaketh of: *No man can do any thing of himself, except it be given him from above.* <note>John 3:27. The author suggests that magical talent is an innate, divinely granted spiritual gift rather than a learned trade.</note>

Every **Character** <term>Character</term> <note>A sacred seal or sigil given by a spirit to a magician to act as a "telephone number" or badge of authority for future summons.</note> given from a Spirit, for what cause soever, hath his efficacy in this business, for which it is given, in the time prefixed: But it is to be used the same day and **Planetary hour** <term>Planetary hour</term> <note>An ancient system where each hour of the day and night is ruled by one of the seven traditional planets.</note> wherein it is given.

7. God liveth, and thy soul liveth: keep thy Covenant, and thou hast whatsoever the Spirit shall reveal unto thee in God, because all things shall be done which the Spirit promiseth unto thee.

->### Aphorism 18.<-

There are other names of the **Olympic spirits** delivered by others; but they only are effectual, which are delivered to any one, by the Spirit the revealer, visible or invisible: and they are delivered to every one as they are predestinated: therefore they are called **Constellations**; and they seldom have any efficacy above 40 years. Therefore it is most safe for the young practitioners of Art, that they work by the offices of the Spirits alone, without their names; and if they are pre-ordained to attain the Art of Magick, the other parts of the Art will offer themselves unto them of their own accord.

# Arbatel of Magick.

Pray therefore for a constant faith, and God will bring to pass all things in due season.

->*Aphorism 19.*<-

**Olympus** <note>In the context of the *Arbatel*, Olympus refers not to the Greek mountain, but to the "Olympic Spirits" who govern the world under divine providence.</note> and the inhabitants thereof do of their own accord offer themselves to men in the forms of Spirits; and they are ready to perform their offices for them, whether they are requested or not. How much more, then, will they attend to you if they are desired?

But there do appear also evil spirits and destroyers; this is caused by the envy and malice of the devil, and because men allure and draw these spirits unto themselves through their sins, as a punishment due to sinners. Whosoever, therefore, desires to have familiar conversation <note>In this historical context, "conversation" means close association, habitual interaction, or living alongside, rather than just talking.</note> with spirits, let him keep himself from all enormous sins and diligently pray to the Most High to be his keeper; then he shall break through all the snares and impediments of the devil. Let him apply himself to the service of God, and God will give him an increase in wisdom.

->*Aphorism 20.*<-

All things are possible to those who believe them and are willing to receive them; but to the incredulous and unwilling, all things are impossible. There is no greater hindrance than a wavering mind, levity <note>Frivolity or a lack of serious intent.</note>, inconstancy, foolish babbling, drunkenness, lusts, and disobedience to the word of God. A Magician <note>original: "Magitian"</note> therefore ought to be a man who is godly, honest, and constant in his words and deeds; he must have a firm faith toward God, and be prudent and covetous <note>Desirous.</note> of nothing but of wisdom regarding divine things.

->*Aphorism 21.*<-

When you would call any of the **Olympic Spirits**, observe the rising of the Sun that day, and the nature of the Spirit which you desire; <note>Each Olympic Spirit is associated with a specific planet and day of the week; the timing of the ritual must align with these celestial correspondences.</note> and by saying the prayer following, your desires shall be perfected.

->*Prayer for a Spirit*<-

Almighty and eternal God, who has ordained the whole creation for your praise and glory, and for the salvation of humankind, I beg you that you would send your Spirit [Name] <note>The text uses "N. N." as a placeholder for the name of the specific spirit being called.</note> of the solar order <note>Spirits in the Arbatel are categorized by the seven wandering stars of ancient astronomy; the "solar order" refers to spirits governed by the Sun.</note>, who shall inform and teach me those things which I shall ask of him; or, that he may bring me medicine against dropsy <term>dropsy: an archaic medical term for edema, or the painful swelling of the body caused by fluid retention</term>, etc. Nevertheless, let not my will be done, but yours, through Jesus Christ your only begotten Son, our Lord. Amen.

But you shall not detain the Spirit for more than a full hour, unless he is familiarly devoted to you.

->*The Dismissal*<-

Since you came in peace and quiet, and have answered my petitions, I give thanks to God, in whose Name you came. And now you may depart in peace to your own orders; and return to me again when I shall call you by your name, or by your order, or by your office, which is granted from the Creator. Amen.

# The third Septenary. <note>A "Septenary" is a group of seven. The Arbatel is organized into sets of seven aphorisms or rules.</note>

## Aphorism 22.

We call that a *secret* which no man can attain to by *human industry without revelation*; this *Science lies obscured, hidden by God in the creature*; which he nevertheless does permit to be revealed by *Spirits*, for a *proper use of the thing itself*. And these *secrets* are either concerning things *divine, natural,* or *human*. But you may *examine a few* and the *most select*, which you will find lead to *many more*.

# Arbatel of Magic.

### Aphorism 23.

Begin your exploration into the nature of a secret mystery either through a Spirit appearing in human form, or through spiritual forces <note>original: "vertues separate" — these are powers or essences acting independently of physical matter</note>, whether through human means or by whatever other way it may be achieved. Once you understand this, ask a Spirit who knows that specific art to explain the secret to you briefly. Pray to God that He would inspire you with His grace, so that you might bring this secret to the conclusion you desire, for the praise and glory of God and for the benefit of your neighbor.

---

### Aphorism 24.

->*The seven greatest secrets.*<-

1. The first is the curing of all diseases within seven days, whether by using symbolic characters <term>characters: occult symbols or sigils believed to hold specific spiritual power</term>, natural substances, or through higher Spirits with divine assistance.
2. The second is the ability to prolong physical and natural life to any age we wish.
<margin>* Spirits of the four elements, according to Paracelsus <note>Paracelsus (1493–1541) was a famous Swiss physician and alchemist who categorized elemental spirits like gnomes and sylphs.</note></margin>
3. The third is to have the obedience of the creatures dwelling in the elements which appear in the form of personal Spirits; these include Pigmies <term>Pigmies: in this tradition, these are Gnomes or spirits of the Earth</term>, Sagani <term>Sagani: a term used by Paracelsus to describe elemental spirits, often those associated with the air</term>, Nymphs, Dryads, and spirits of the woods.
4. The fourth is the ability to speak with wisdom and understanding about all things visible and invisible, and to understand the power and purpose of every individual thing.
5. The fifth is for a person to be able to govern themselves according to the purpose for which God has appointed them.
6. The sixth is to know God, Christ, and His Holy Spirit: this is the perfection of the Microcosm <term>Microcosm: the concept that a human being is a "little world" that perfectly mirrors the structure and laws of the entire universe (the macrocosm)</term>.
7. The seventh is to be spiritually reborn <note>original: "regenerate"</note>, like Enoch <note>original: "Henochius" — the biblical patriarch Enoch was famously "taken by God" and was believed in occult traditions to have mastered all earthly and divine secrets.</note>, the King of the lower world <note>In this context, the "inferior" or lower world refers to the material, earthly realm inhabited by humans.</note>.

These seven secrets a man of an honest and constant
->mind<-

# Arbatel of Magic.

...mind may learn from the Spirits, without any offense toward God.

->*The intermediate secrets are likewise seven in number.*<-

1. The first is the transformation of metals, which is commonly called *Alchemy*; this is certainly granted to very few, and only by special grace. <note>original: "transmutation of Metals"</note>
2. The second is the curing of diseases with metals, either by the magnetic virtues <term>virtues: the inherent medicinal or spiritual powers believed to reside within physical objects</term> of precious stones, or by the use of the Philosopher's Stone, and similar means.
3. The third is to be able to perform astronomical and mathematical miracles, such as *hydraulic engines* <term>hydraulic engines: machines powered by water; in the 16th century, complex water-works and automatons were considered wonders of science and "mathematical magic"</term>, and to conduct business by the influence of Heaven <note>This refers to elective astrology—choosing the best time for actions based on planetary alignments</note>, and other things of this sort.
4. The fourth is to perform the works of natural magic, of whatever kind they may be.
5. The fifth is to know all physical secrets.
6. The sixth is to know the foundation of all arts which are practiced with the hands and the labor of the body.
7. The seventh is to know the foundation of all arts which are practiced by the angelic nature of man. <note>The "angelic nature" refers to the human intellect and soul, which Renaissance thinkers believed shared the same essence as celestial beings.</note>

->*The lesser secrets are seven.*<-

1. The first is to do a thing diligently and to accumulate much wealth.
2. The second is to rise from a humble state to positions of dignity and honor, and to establish a new family line which may be illustrious and achieve great things.
3. The third is to excel in military affairs, to successfully achieve great deeds, and to be a leader among the leaders of Kings and Princes.
4. The fourth is to be a good householder both in the Country and the City. <note>original: "house-keeper" — meaning a steward or manager of an estate</note>
5. The fifth is to be an industrious and fortunate Merchant.

->Aphorism 25.<-

We have already declared what a secret is, along with its kinds and species; it remains now to show how we may attain the knowledge of those things which we desire.

The true and only way to all secrets is to have recourse unto God, the Author of all good; and as Christ teaches, *In the first place seek ye the kingdom of God and his righteousness, and all these things shall be added unto you.* <note>original: Matthew 6:33</note>

2. *Also see that your hearts be not burdened with surfeiting <term>surfeiting: overindulgence in food or drink</term>, and drunkenness, and the cares of this life.* <note>original: Luke 21:34</note>

7. *If you will do the will of my Father which is in heaven, ye are truly my disciples, and we will come unto you, and make our abode with you.* <note>original: John 14:23</note>

If you draw these seven places of Scripture from the literal word into the Spirit, or into action, you cannot err, but shall attain to the desired goal; you shall not miss the mark, and God himself by his holy Spirit will teach you true and profitable things: he will also give his ministering Angels <term>ministering Angels: celestial beings believed to act as messengers and helpers to humanity on behalf of the Divine</term> unto you, to be your companions, helpers, and teachers of all the secrets of the world, and he will command every creature to be obedient unto you, so that cheerfully

### Aphorism 25.

There is another way which is more common, so that secrets may be revealed unto thee also when thou art unwitting <term>unwitting: unaware or without conscious effort</term> thereof, either by God, or by Spirits which have secrets in their power; or by dreams, or by strong imaginations and impressions, or by the constellation of a nativity by celestial knowledge. <note>This refers to "innate" or "natural" magic, where knowledge comes through astrological influence at birth or sudden divine inspiration.</note> After this manner are made heroic men <term>heroic men: in Renaissance philosophy, "heroes" were those whose souls were exceptionally attuned to the divine or the celestial spheres</term>, such as there are very many, and all learned men in the world: *Plato, Aristotle, Hippocrates, Galen, Euclid, Archimedes, Hermes Trismegistus* the father of secrets, along with *Theophrastus Paracelsus*; all which men had in themselves all the virtues of secrets. Hitherto also are referred *Homer, Hesiod, Orpheus, Pythagoras*; but these had not such gifts of secrets as the former. To this are also referred the Nymphs, and sons of *Melusina*, <note>Melusina is a figure of European folklore, a water-spirit; Paracelsus claimed such elemental beings could intermarry with humans.</note> and the Gods of the Gentiles, *Achilles, Aeneas, Hercules*; also *Cyrus, Alexander the Great, Julius Caesar, Lucullus, Sylla, Marius*.

It is a canon <term>canon: a fundamental rule or principle</term>, that every one know his own Angel, and that he obey him according to the Word of God; and let him beware of the snares of the evil Angel, lest he be involved in the calamities of *Brutus* and *Mark Antony*. <note>Brutus and Mark Antony were famously said to have been visited by "evil genii" or spirits that foretold their dooms at Philippi and through their downfall.</note> To this, refer to the book of *Jovianus Pontanus* regarding Fortune, and his *Eutichus*. <note>Giovanni Gioviano Pontano (1429–1503) was a humanist who wrote on the role of fate and individual character.</note>

The third way is diligent and hard labor, without which no great thing can be obtained from the divine Deity worthy of admiration, as it is said:

> Nothing shalt thou say or do against Minerva’s will.
> <note>original Latin: "Tu nihil invita dices faciésve Minerva." A quote from Horace's *Ars Poetica*, meaning that one should not attempt anything for which they have no natural talent or which lacks divine favor (Minerva being the goddess of wisdom).</note>

# Arbatel of Magic.

We do detest all evil magicians, who make themselves associates with the devils through their unlawful superstitions, and who obtain and effect some things which God permits to be done as a punishment of the devils. So also they perform other evil acts, the devil being the author, as the Scriptures testify concerning *Judas*. To these are referred all idolaters of old, and of our own age, and abusers of Fortune, such as the heathens are full of. And to these also appertain all Charontic <term>Charontic: derived from Charon, the ferryman of the dead in Greek mythology; refers to necromancy or the summoning of spirits from the underworld</term> evocation of spirits, such as the work of *Saul* with the woman, <note>A reference to King Saul and the Witch of Endor in 1 Samuel 28.</note> and *Lucan's* prophecy of the deceased soldier concerning the outcome of the Pharsalian war, <note>Marcus Annaeus Lucanus (Lucan) was a Roman poet whose epic "Pharsalia" describes a gruesome scene where a witch reanimates a corpse to predict the future.</note> and the like.

---

### Aphorism 27.

Make a Circle with a center A, which is B. C. D. E. At the East let there be B. C. a square. At the North, C. D. At the West, D. E. And at the South, E. D. Divide the several quadrants into seven parts, so that there may be in the whole 28 parts: and let them be again divided into four parts, so that there may be 112 parts of the Circle: and so many are the true secrets to be revealed. And this Circle, divided in this manner, is the seal of the secrets of the world, which they draw from the only center A, that is, from the invisible God, unto the whole of creation.

The Prince of the Oriental <note>original: "Orientalis," meaning Eastern</note> secrets is resident in the middle, and has three Nobles on either side, every one of whom has four under him, and the Prince himself has four appertaining unto him. And in this manner the other Princes and Nobles have their quadrants of secrets, each with their four secrets. 

But the Eastern secret is the study of all wisdom; the West, of strength; the South, of tillage <note>original: "cultura," referring here to agriculture and the cultivation of the earth</note>; the North, of a more rigid life. So it is that the Eastern secrets are commended as the best; the Meridian <note>original: "Meridiana," meaning South</note> to be middling; and the West and North to be lesser. 

The use of this seal of secrets is so that you may know from where the Spirits or Angels are produced, who may teach the secrets delivered unto them from God. But they have names taken from their offices and powers, according to the gift 

<meta>The text cuts off here, continuing with the catchword "gift" for the following page.</meta>

...gift which God has individually distributed to each of them. One has the power of the sword; another, of pestilence; and another, of inflicting famine upon the people, as ordained by God. Some are destroyers of cities, as were those two who were sent to overthrow *Sodom* and *Gomorrah* and the surrounding places, as the Holy Scriptures testify. Some are watchmen over kingdoms; others are the guardians of private individuals. From this, anyone may easily derive their names in his own language.

Thus, whoever wishes may request an angel of physics <note>In this context, "physics" refers to natural science and medicine.</note>, mathematics, or philosophy; or an angel of civil wisdom, or of supernatural or natural wisdom, or for anything whatsoever. Let him ask seriously, with a great desire of his mind, and with faith and constancy; and without doubt, he shall receive what he asks for from the Father and God of all spirits. This faith surpasses all seals and brings them into subjection to the will of man. 

The characteristic method <note>Refers to the "Characters" or symbolic sigils used in ritual magic to call upon specific entities.</note> of calling angels follows this faith, and it depends solely on divine revelation; but without this preceding faith, the method remains in obscurity. Nevertheless, if anyone uses these methods simply as a memorial, and for no other reason—viewing them as something created by God for a specific purpose to which a spiritual power or essence is bound—he may use them without offending God. 

But let him beware lest he fall into idolatry and the snares of the devil, who easily deceives the unwary with his cunning sorceries. The devil is not overcome except by the "finger of God" <note>A biblical idiom for divine power, used in Exodus and by Jesus in the Gospels.</note> and is appointed to the service of humanity. Thus, the fallen spirits serve the godly unwillingly, yet not without bringing temptations and tribulations, because the commandment states that the serpent shall bruise the heel of Christ, the seed of woman. <note>A reference to the "Protevangelium" in Genesis 3:15, often interpreted as the ongoing struggle between humanity/Christ and the devil.</note>

We must therefore practice spiritual matters with fear and trembling, and with great reverence toward God, and interact with spiritual essences with gravity and justice. Whoever meddles with such things, let him beware of all levity, pride, greed, vanity, envy, and ungodliness, lest he perish miserably.

### Aphorism 28.

Because all good comes from God, who alone is good, those things which we wish to obtain from him should be sought through prayer in Spirit and Truth, and with a simple heart. The conclusion of the **secret of secrets** is this: let everyone practice prayer for the things they desire, and they shall not suffer a rejection. Let no one despise prayer; for to whoever prays to God, He is both able and willing to give. Let us now acknowledge Him as the Author, from whom we humbly seek our desires. A merciful and good Father loves "children of desires" <note>This is a reference to the biblical prophet Daniel, who is called "a man of desires" (Daniel 9:23 and 10:11) in the Vulgate translation—meaning one who is greatly beloved and earnestly seeks God.</note>, like ***Daniel***; and He hears us sooner than we are able to overcome the hardness of our hearts to pray. However, He does not wish for us to "give holy things to dogs" <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6; a warning against sharing sacred knowledge with those who would disrespect or misuse it.</note>, nor to despise and treat the gifts of His treasury with contempt. Therefore, diligently and frequently read the first **Septenary** <note>A "Septenary" is a group or set of seven; here it refers to the first seven aphorisms of this book.</note> of secrets, and guide and direct your life and all your thoughts according to those precepts; and all things shall yield to the desires of your mind in the Lord, in whom you trust.

## The fifth Septenary.

### Aphorism 29.

As our study of Magic proceeds in order from the general rules already stated, let us now come to a specific explanation of them. **Spirits** are either divine ministers of the Word, of the Church, and its members; or else they are servants to the Creatures <note>In this context, "Creatures" refers to all created physical things in the universe.</note> in physical matters—partly for the salvation of the soul and body, and partly for its destruction. And nothing is done, whether good or evil, without a certain and determined order and government. He who seeks a good end, let him follow it; and he who desires an evil end also pursues it, and does so earnestly, resulting in divine punishment and a turning away from the divine will.

<summary>This page emphasizes that prayer and a pure heart are the "secret of secrets" for obtaining divine favor, while also beginning a new section that classifies spirits as either divine ministers or servants of the physical world.</summary>

# Arbatel of Magic.

Therefore, let everyone compare their goals with the word of God, using it as a touchstone that will judge between good and evil. Let them decide for themselves what should be avoided and what should be sought after; and once they have determined their path, let them follow it diligently, without procrastination or delay, until they reach their appointed goal.

->Aphorism 30.<-

Those who desire riches, worldly glory, high office, honors, dignities, or absolute power—and seek these through magical means—will obtain them if they strive diligently. Each shall receive according to their destiny, their hard work, and their knowledge of the magical sciences. This is evidenced by the history of *Melusine* <note>Melusine is a figure of European folklore, a feminine spirit of fresh water in sacred springs and rivers. She is often depicted as a woman who is a serpent or fish from the waist down and is associated with the founding of noble houses, such as the House of Lusignan.</note> and the magicians associated with her. They decreed that no member of the Italian nation should ever again rule the Kingdom of *Naples*, and they brought it to pass that the ruler of that age was cast down from his throne. Such is the great power of the guardian or "tutelar" <note>From the Latin *tutela*, meaning a protector or guardian deity of a specific place or person.</note> angels of the kingdoms of the world.

->Aphorism 31.<-

Summon the Prince of the Kingdom and issue a command to him, and whatever you wish shall be done—provided that the Prince is not later released from his obedience by a succeeding magician. Therefore, the Kingdom of *Naples* could be restored to the Italians if a magician were to summon the one who originally established this order and compel him to revoke his deed. A magician could also be compelled to restore the secret powers taken from the treasury of Magic: namely, a Book, a Gem, and a magical Horn. <note>These three items—a book of knowledge, a jewel of power, and a horn of summoning—are classic "regalia" or ritual tools often mentioned in medieval and Renaissance grimoires as symbols of absolute magical authority.</note> If someone possessed these, they could easily, if they wished, make themselves the monarch of the world. But *the Jew* <note>original: "Judæus." This likely refers to a legendary figure in magical literature, possibly "Techel the Jew," who was famously associated with the lore of engraved gemstones and their virtues.</note> chose instead to live among the gods until the final judgment, preferring that over the fleeting goods of this world. His heart is so blinded that he understands nothing of the God of heaven and earth, nor thinks of Him, but instead enjoys the delights of immortal things to...
