# Arbatel of Magic.

...his own eternal destruction. And he may be summoned more easily than the Angel of *Plotinus* in the Temple of *Isis* <note>The philosopher Plotinus (c. 204–270 AD) was said by his biographer Porphyry to have had a guardian spirit so high in rank that it was called an "angel" or "god." This spirit supposedly appeared when an Egyptian priest summoned it in the Temple of Isis in Rome.</note>.

### Aphorism 32.

In a similar way, the Romans were instructed by the *Sibylline* books <note>The Sibylline Books were a collection of oracular utterances bought from a prophetess (a Sibyl) by the last king of Rome and consulted by the Senate in times of crisis.</note>; and through those means, they became the masters of the world, as history testifies. However, the lords who serve the Prince of a Kingdom bestow the lesser government offices <term>magistracies: administrative or judicial offices</term>. Therefore, he who desires a lower office or rank should use magic to call upon a Noble serving that Prince, and his desire shall be fulfilled.

### Aphorism 33.

But he who craves less honorable status, such as wealth alone, let him call upon the Prince of Riches or one of his lords, and he shall obtain his desire in whatever form he wishes to grow wealthy—whether through earthly goods, trade, the gifts of rulers, or through the study of *Metals* or *Chemistry* <note>original: "Chymistry," which in this 16th-century context refers to alchemy—the art of transmuting base metals into gold.</note>. As the magician presents any method of growing rich by these means, he shall achieve his goal.

### Aphorism 34.

Every kind of summoning <note>original: "evocation," the act of calling forth a spirit</note> follows the same nature and form; this method was familiar in ancient times to the *Sibyls* and the high priests. In our own time, through ignorance and lack of piety, this knowledge is completely lost; and that which remains has been corrupted by endless lies and superstitions.

### Aphorism 35.

Human understanding is the sole driver of all wonderful works, provided it is joined to a Spirit; and once joined, the mind produces whatever she wills. Therefore, we must proceed carefully in *Magic*, lest *Sirens* and other...

## The sixth Septenary <note>A "Septenary" is a group of seven; the *Arbatel* is organized into seven groups of seven aphorisms each.</note>.

->Aphorism 36.<-

Care must be taken that experiments are not mixed with other experiments, but that each remains simple and distinct. For God and Nature have ordained all things for a specific and appointed purpose. For example, those who perform healings using the most basic herbs and roots <term>simples: medicinal plants used on their own, rather than in complex mixtures</term> achieve the most successful results. In this same way, the greatest influences and powers <term>vertues: the inherent natural or spiritual power of an object</term>—which are like miracles—lie hidden within the Stars <note>original: "Constellations"</note>, Words, Characters, and Stones.

Likewise, there are certain words which, when spoken, immediately cause both visible and invisible creatures to obey. These include creatures of our own world, as well as those of the water, the air, the subterranean depths, and the Olympic <note>In this context, "Olympick" refers to the celestial or planetary spirits associated with the heavens.</note>, super-celestial, infernal, and even divine realms.

Therefore, simplicity should be the primary focus of study, and knowledge of such "simples" should be sought from God; for they cannot be discovered by any other means or experience.

->Aphorism 37.<-

And let all things be kept in their proper place. Order, Reason, and Method <note>original: "Means"</note> are the three things that make all learning—whether of visible or invisible creatures—easy to understand. This is the path of Order: recognizing that some creatures are creatures of

# Arbatel of Magic.

the light <note>This text continues from the previous page, describing the "Olympic" or celestial spirits.</note>; others, of darkness: these are subject to vanity, because they run headlong into darkness, and enthrall themselves in eternal punishments for their rebellion. Their Kingdom is partly very beautiful in transitory and corruptible things on the one hand, because it cannot exist without some virtue <term>virtue: here meaning inherent power or divine energy</term> and great gifts of God; and partly most filthy and horrid to be spoken of, because it abounds with all wickedness and sin, idolatry, contempt of God, blasphemies against the true God and his works, worshipping of devils, disobedience towards Magistrates <note>Refers to civil authorities and government officials.</note>, seditions, homicides, robberies, tyranny, adulteries, wicked lusts, rapes, thefts, lies, perjuries, pride, and a covetous desire of rule. 

In this mixture consists the kingdom of darkness: but the creatures of the light are filled with eternal truth and with the grace of God; they are Lords of the whole world, and reign over the Lords of darkness, as the members of Christ. Between these and the others, there is a continual war, until God shall put an end to their strife by his last judgment.

->*Aphorism 38.*<-

Therefore *Magic* <term>Magic: in this context, the sacred science of interacting with spiritual beings</term> is twofold in its first division. The one is of God, which he bestows on the creatures of light; the other also is of God, but it is the gift which he gives unto the creatures of darkness. This second type is also twofold: the first is for a good end, as when the Princes of darkness are compelled to do good unto the creatures because God enforces them; the other is for an evil end, when God permits such spirits to punish evil persons, so that they are magically deceived to their own destruction; or, he also commands such spirits to be cast out into destruction.

The second division of *Magic* is that it brings certain works to pass using visible instruments through visible things; it effects other works with invisible instruments by invisible things; and it performs other things using a mixture of both means, instruments, and effects.

<summary>This page describes the spiritual warfare between the kingdoms of light and darkness and defines magic as a divinely regulated power that can be used for either good or judgment. It categorizes magic into two main branches: one based on the nature of the spirits involved and another based on whether the tools used are visible or invisible.</summary>

<keywords>creatures of light, kingdom of darkness, spiritual warfare, Aphorism 38, divine permission, visible and invisible instruments, dualism, judgment</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the categorization of magic begun on page 206 under Aphorism 38. It completes the seven-fold division of magical practice and concludes by ranking the different forms of the art based on their spiritual merit.</meta>

# Arbatel of Magic.

The third division is: some things are brought to pass by the invocation <term>invocation: the act of calling upon a deity or spirit for aid or inspiration</term> of God alone. This is partly Prophetical and Philosophical; and partly, as it were, Theophrastical <term>Theophrastical: relating to the mystical philosophy of Paracelsus, which emphasizes the divine power residing within nature and the human soul</term>.

Other things there are which, by reason of the ignorance of the true God, are done with the Princes of Spirits so that a person's desires may be fulfilled; such is the work of the Mercurialists <note>Mercurialists likely refers to those seeking swift results, wealth, or secret knowledge through intermediate spirits rather than divine grace—named after the god or planet Mercury.</note>.

The fourth division is that some exercise their Magic with the good Angels instead of God, as it were descending down from the most high God; such was the Magic of Baalim <note>original: "Baalim." This is the plural of the Hebrew word "Baal," meaning "Lord" or "Master." In this context, it refers to those who worked with planetary "lords" or angelic intermediaries.</note>.

Another Magic is that which carries out its actions with the chief of the evil Spirits; such were those who wrought by the minor Gods of the heathens.

The fifth division is that some act with Spirits openly and face to face, which is given to few; others work by dreams and other signs, which the ancients took from their auguries <term>auguries: the ancient practice of interpreting omens, such as the flight of birds or the appearance of entrails, to predict the future</term> and sacrifices.

The sixth division is that some work by immortal creatures, while others work by mortal creatures such as Nymphs, Satyrs, and similar inhabitants of the other elements—Pigmies <note>In the elemental philosophy of the Renaissance, "Pigmies" (or Gnomes) were considered the mortal but long-lived spirits inhabiting the element of Earth.</note>, and so on.

The seventh division is that the Spirits serve some of their own accord, without any art; others they will scarcely attend to, even when called by art <term>art: the formal system of rituals, symbols, and constraints used in ceremonial magic</term>.

Among these species of Magic, that which depends upon God alone is the most excellent of all. The second is that of those whom the Spirits serve faithfully of their own accord. The third is that which is the property of Christians, which depends on the power of Christ which he has in heaven and earth.

<summary>This page outlines the final five divisions of magic, distinguishing between divine invocation, the use of angelic or elemental intermediaries, and the use of "art" versus spontaneous spiritual service.</summary>

# Arbatel of Magic.

### Aphorism 39.

->*There is a seven-fold preparation to learn the*<-
->*Magic Art.*<-

The first is to meditate day and night on how to attain the true knowledge of God, both by his word revealed from the foundation of the world, and also by the <term>seal of the creation: the idea that God’s "signature" or divine patterns can be seen imprinted upon the natural world</term> and his creatures; and by the wonderful effects which the visible and invisible creatures of God show forth.

Secondly, it is requisite that a man descend down into himself, and chiefly study to know himself; what mortal part he has in him, and what immortal; and what part is proper to himself, and what is <note>original: "diverse." In this context, it refers to external influences acting upon the soul, such as spiritual or planetary forces.</note> diverse.

Thirdly, that he learn by the immortal part of himself to worship, love, and fear the eternal God, and to adore him in Spirit and Truth; and with his mortal part, to do those things which he knows to be acceptable to God, and profitable to his neighbors.

These are the three first and chiefest precepts of <term>Magic: here referring to "Theosophia" or divine wisdom, rather than common sorcery</term>, wherewith let every one prepare himself that desires to obtain true Magic or divine wisdom, so that he may be accounted worthy thereof, and one to whom the Angelic creatures willingly do service, not only in secret but also manifestly, and as it were face to face.

Fourthly, whereas every man is to be vigilant to see to what kind of life he shall be called from his mother's womb, let every one know whether he be born to Magic, and to what <note>original: "species thereof." The author suggests that individuals are naturally inclined toward specific branches of spiritual study, such as healing, prophecy, or communication with spirits.</note> species thereof. This is something every one may perceive easily by reading these things and by finding success through experience; for such things and such gifts are not given except only to the low and humble.

In the fifth place, we are to take care that we understand when the Spirits are assisting us in undertaking the greatest business; and he that understands this, it is manifest,

# Arbatel of Magic.

...manifest, that he shall be made a Magician by the <note>original: "ordination of God." This implies that true magical skill is a divine appointment or calling, rather than a skill one can simply seize.</note> appointment of God; that is, a person who employs the ministry of Spirits to achieve excellent results. In this, most people sin—either through negligence, ignorance, or contempt, or through excessive superstition. They also offend through ingratitude toward God, which has led many famous men to their own destruction. They sin by being rash and stubborn, and by failing to use their gifts for the honor of God as required, preferring instead <margin>curious or meddling works <note>original Greek: πέριεργα ἔργοις (*perierga ergois*); a term used in the New Testament (Acts 19:19) to refer to "superfluous" or "curious" magical arts that are unauthorized or vain.</note></margin>.

Sixthly, the Magician requires faith and <term>taciturnity: the habit of keeping silence, especially regarding sacred secrets or private spiritual revelations.</term> Above all, he must not disclose any secret that the Spirit has forbidden him to reveal. Just as the prophet *Daniel* was commanded to "seal" certain things—that is, not to declare them publicly—so it was not lawful for *Paul* to speak openly of all the things he saw in his vision. No one will believe how much is contained within this single instruction.

Seventhly, in one who wishes to be a Magician, the greatest justice is required. He must undertake nothing that is ungodly, wicked, or unjust, nor even allow such thoughts to enter his mind; in this way, he shall be divinely protected from all evil.

->*Aphorism 40.*<-

When the Magician decides to perform any <note>original: "incorporeal thing." This refers to spiritual work or interactions with beings that do not have physical bodies, utilizing either the physical senses or the "interior" senses of the soul.</note> incorporeal act, whether using his external or internal senses, he must govern himself according to the following laws to achieve his magical goal.

The first Law is this: let him know that such a Spirit is assigned to him by God. Let him meditate on the fact that God sees all his thoughts and actions; therefore, let him direct the entire course of his life according to the rules prescribed in the Word of God.

Secondly, always pray with *David*: *“Take not thy holy Spirit from me; and strengthen me with thy free Spirit; and lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil. I beseech thee, O heavenly Father, do not give power to any lying Spirit, as thou”*

# Arbatel of Magic.

...didst over Ahab that he perished; but keep me in thy truth. *Amen.* <note>Ahab: a reference to the biblical King Ahab, who was misled by a "lying spirit" to his downfall, as described in 1 Kings 22.</note>

Thirdly, Let him accustom himself to try the Spirits, as the Scripture admonishes; for grapes cannot be gathered from thorns: let us try all things, and hold fast that which is good and laudable, so that we may avoid everything that is repugnant to the divine power.

The fourth is, To be remote and clear from all manner of superstition; for this is superstition: to attribute divinity in this place to things in which there is nothing divine at all; or to choose or frame for ourselves a way to worship God with some kind of worship which he has not commanded. Such are the Magical ceremonies of Satan, whereby he impudently offers himself to be worshipped as God.

The fifth thing to be eschewed is all worship of Idols, which binds any divine power to idols or other things of their own proper motion, where they are not placed by the Creator or by the order of Nature: things which many false and wicked Magicians feign.

Sixthly, All the deceitful imitations and affections of the devil are also to be avoided, whereby he imitates the power of the creation and of the Creator, so that he may produce things with a word in such a way that they may not be what they truly are. This belongs only to the <term>Omnipotency: the all-powerful nature of God.</term> of God and is not communicable to any created being.

Seventhly, Let us cleave fast to the gifts of God and of his holy Spirit, so that we may know them and diligently embrace them with our whole heart and all our strength.

->*Aphorism 41.*<-

We come now to the nine last Aphorisms of this whole Tome; wherewith we will, the divine mercy assisting us, conclude this whole Magical <term>Isagoge: an introductory manual or scholarly primer.</term> <note>original: "Isagoge"; from the Greek *eisagoge*, meaning an introduction to a subject.</note>

Therefore, in the first place, it is to be observed what we understand by "Magician" in this work.

This description of a Magician is clearly evident and universal.

An evil Magician is one whom evil Spirits serve—by divine permission—leading to his earthly and eternal ruin and destruction. His purpose is to deceive people and pull them away from God. Such a person was *Simon Magus* <note>A biblical figure often called "the father of all heresies," known for practicing sorcery and trying to buy spiritual power.</note>, who is mentioned in the *Acts of the Apostles* and in the writings of *Clement*; Saint *Peter* commanded him to be cast down to the earth after he had ordered himself to be lifted into the air by unclean Spirits as if he were a god.

Secondly, we must understand that a Magician is a person destined for this work from their mother's womb. ## The Seventh Septenary

->Aphorism 43<-

The Lord lives, and the works of God live in Him by His appointment, through which He wills them to exist; for He desires them to use their liberty in obedience to His commands, or in disobedience to them. To those who are obedient, He has proposed rewards; to the disobedient, He has set forth their deserved punishment. 

Therefore, these Spirits—out of their own free will and through their pride and contempt for the Son of God—have revolted against God their Creator and are reserved for the day of wrath. Despite this, a very great power remains in them over the creation; nevertheless, it is limited, and they are confined within their boundaries by the "bridle" of God. 

Therefore, the <term>Magician of God</term> <note>original: "Magitian of God"; defined here as a "wise man of God" or one divinely instructed.</note> is led by the hand of God toward all everlasting good, including both humble matters and the most significant <term>corporal</term> <note>Physical or bodily things, as opposed to spiritual ones.</note> things.

The power of Satan is great because of the great sins of men. Therefore, the magicians of Satan also perform great things, greater than any man would believe. Although they remain within their own limits, they are nevertheless beyond all human understanding regarding the physical and transitory things of this life, as many ancient histories and daily examples testify. Both kinds of Magic differ from one another in their goals: the one leads to eternal good and uses temporal things with...

->Aphorism 44.<-

The transition from the common life of man to a magical life is nothing more than a sleep to the former life and an awakening to this life. For those things which happen to ignorant and unwise men in their everyday life, the same things happen to the willing and informed Magician.

The Magician understands when his own mind is meditating; he deliberates, reasons, decides, and determines what must be done. He observes when his thoughts <note>original: "cogitations"</note> proceed from a <term>divine separate essence</term> <note>In Renaissance philosophy, this refers to a spiritual intelligence or angel that exists independently of the human soul.</note>, and he tests the order and nature of that divine essence.

But the man who is ignorant of Magic is tossed to and fro, as if in a war with his own emotions. He does not know when these thoughts originate from his own mind or are impressed upon him by an <term>assisting spirit</term> <note>A spiritual entity assigned to help or influence a human.</note>; and he does not know how to defeat the plans of his enemies with the word of God, or how to protect himself from the traps and deceptions of the tempter.

->Aphorism 45.<-

The greatest precept of Magic is to know what every man ought to receive for his use from the assisting Spirit, and what to refuse. This he may learn from the Psalmist, who says:

> *How shall a young man keep his way pure? By guarding it according to Your word, O Lord.* <note>A translation of Psalm 119:9.</note>

To keep the word of God, so that the evil one cannot snatch it out of the heart, is the highest teaching of wisdom. It is lawful to accept and practice other suggestions that are not contrary to the glory of God and charity toward our neighbors, without asking from what Spirit such suggestions proceed. ->Aphorism 46.<-

There is nothing that so becomes a man as constancy in his words and deeds. When like rejoices in like, there are none more happy than such people, because the holy Angels associate with them and take them into their custody. On the contrary, men who are inconstant are lighter than nothing, like rotten leaves. We choose the 46th Aphorism from these truths: Just as every person governs themselves, so they attract to themselves Spirits of their own nature and condition.

However, one advisor very truly warns that no man should carry himself beyond his own calling, lest he draw to himself some malignant Spirit from the outermost parts of the earth, by whom he shall either be driven mad and deceived, or brought to final destruction. This precept appears most plainly in the story of **Midas** <note>In Greek mythology, King Midas was granted the power to turn everything he touched into gold, which eventually led to his near-starvation.</note>: when he wished to convert all things into gold, he drew a Spirit to himself that was able to perform this; but being deceived by that spirit, he would have been brought to death by famine if his foolishness had not been corrected by the mercy of God. 

The same thing happened in our own times to a certain woman near **Frankfurt on the Oder** <note>original: "Franckford at Odera"; a city in Brandenburg, Germany.</note>, who sought to scrape together and devour money from any source. I wish that men would diligently weigh this precept and not dismiss the histories of Midas and similar figures as mere fables. They would be much more diligent in moderating their thoughts and affections, and they would not be so perpetually tormented by the Spirits of the "golden mountains of **Utopia**" <note>A reference to Thomas More’s fictional island; here used to mean chasing imaginary or impossible wealth.</note>. Therefore, we ought most diligently to observe that...

<summary>This page concludes the Seventh Septenary by advising practitioners to prioritize spiritual health over material greed. It warns that a person's inner character attracts corresponding spirits, using the legend of King Midas and local folklore as cautionary tales against avarice.</summary>

# Arbatel of Magic

...such presumptions should be cast out of the mind by the Word <note>Referring to the "Word of God" or Scripture.</note> while they are new; neither let them become a habit in an idle mind that is empty of the divine word.

### Aphorism 47.

He who is faithfully engaged in his <term>vocation</term> <note>A "calling" or one's proper station and duty in life, both professional and spiritual.</note> shall also have Spirits as constant companions of his desires, who will successively supply him in all things. But if he has any knowledge in Magic, they will not be unwilling to show themselves to him, to converse with him familiarly, and to serve him in those various ministries to which they are appointed; the good Spirits in good things, leading toward salvation, and the evil Spirits in every evil thing, leading to destruction. Examples are not lacking in the histories of the whole world, and they happen daily in the world. **Theodosius** <note>Theodosius I (the Great), a Roman Emperor whose victory at the Battle of the Frigidus was traditionally attributed to divine intervention and miraculous winds.</note> before his victory over **Arbogast** <note>A Frankish general who rebelled against Theodosius.</note> is an example of the good; **Brutus** <note>Marcus Junius Brutus, one of the assassins of Julius Caesar.</note> before he was slain is an example of the evil Spirits, when he was haunted by the Spirit of **Caesar** <note>According to Plutarch, Brutus was visited by a "monstrous spirit" or "evil genius" before the Battle of Philippi.</note> and exposed to punishment, so that he killed himself—he who had killed his own Father <note>Brutus was rumored to be the illegitimate son of Caesar.</note> and the **Father of his Country** <note>original: "Pater Patriae"; a Roman honorary title held by Julius Caesar.</note>.

### Aphorism 48.

All Magic is a revelation of Spirits of that specific kind which matches the Magic itself; so the nine Muses are called the "ninth Magic" in **Hesiod** <note>An ancient Greek poet.</note>, as he clearly testifies of himself in the *Theogony* <note>original: "Theogony"; Hesiod’s poem regarding the origins and genealogies of the Greek gods.</note>. In **Homer**, it is the genius of **Ulysses** in the *Psychagogia* <note>original: "Psigiogagia"; Greek for "the conjuring of souls." This refers to the *Nekyia* in Book 11 of the *Odyssey*, where Odysseus (Ulysses) summons spirits from the underworld.</note>. **Hermes** <note>Likely referring to Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of Hermeticism.</note> represents the Spirits of the more sublime parts of the mind. God revealed himself to **Moses** in the bush. The three wise men who came to seek **Christ** at Jerusalem were led by the Angel of the Lord. The Angels of the Lord directed **Daniel**. Therefore, there is nothing of which anyone may boast; for *it is not of him that wills, nor of him that runs, but of him to whom God will show mercy* <note>A paraphrase of Romans 9:16.</note>, or according to some other spiritual fate. From this source all Magic springs, and to that same source it will return, whether it be good or evil. In

The text concludes that all magic is a form of spiritual revelation, emphasizing that success in these arts comes from divine mercy rather than human will.</summary>

# Arbatel of Magic

...son of Caesar. In this manner **Tages**, the first teacher of the Magic of the Romans, gushed out of the earth <note>In Etruscan mythology, Tages was a founding prophet who was miraculously plowed up from a field; he appeared as a child but possessed the wisdom of an old man.</note>. **Diana** of the **Ephesians** showed her worship as if it had been sent from heaven. So also **Apollo**. And all the religion of the Heathens is taken from the same Spirits; neither are the opinions of the **Sadducees** <note>A Jewish sect during the time of Jesus that was noted for its denial of the afterlife and the existence of spirits or angels.</note> merely human inventions.

### Aphorism 49.

The conclusion therefore of this <term>Introduction</term> <note>Original: *Isagoge*, a Greek term for a formal introductory treatise.</note> is the same which we have already spoken of above: That even as there is one God, from whom all good originates; and one sin, namely disobedience against the will of the commanding God, from which all evil comes; so that *the fear of God is the beginning of all wisdom* <note>A foundational biblical proverb from Psalm 111:10 and Proverbs 9:10.</note>, and the profit of all Magic. For obedience to the will of God follows the fear of God; and after this, there follow the presence of God and of the Holy Spirit, and the ministry of the holy Angels, and all good things out of the inexhaustible treasures of God.

But unprofitable and damnable Magic arises from this: where we lose the fear of God out of our hearts and suffer sin to reign in us, there the Prince of this world <note>A common biblical title for Satan, emphasizing his temporary influence over earthly affairs.</note>, the god of this world, begins and sets up his kingdom instead of holy things, in such people as he finds profitable for his kingdom. There, even as the spider takes the fly which falls into his web, so **Satan** spreads abroad his nets and takes men with the snares of covetousness, until he sucks them dry and draws them to eternal fire. These he cherishes and advances on high, so that their fall may be the greater.

Courteous Reader, apply your eyes and mind to the sacred and profane histories, and to those things which you see daily to be done in the world, and you shall find all things full of Magic, according to a two-fold science, good and evil. So that these may be better discerned, we will put here their division and subdivision for the conclusion of these <term>Introductions</term>; wherein everyone may contemplate what is to be followed and what is to be avoided, and how far one must labor toward a competent end of life and living.

<summary>This page concludes the introductory section (Isagoge) of the Arbatel by distinguishing between divine magic, rooted in the fear of God, and damnable magic, which stems from sin and the deceptions of Satan.</summary>
<keywords>Tages, Diana of the Ephesians, Sadducees, Aphorism 49, Isagoge, fear of God, Satan, Prince of this world, spiritual discernment, good and evil magic</keywords>

--- Page 233 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Isagoge" or introduction to the Arbatel of Magic. It completes the final aphorism regarding the origins of good and evil magic and provides a concluding classification of knowledge.</meta>

...the Prince of this world <note>A common biblical title for Satan, emphasizing his temporary influence over the material realm.</note>, the captain of darkness, begins to establish his kingdom and to tempt us with his arts; and because we have turned away from the true light, we fall into the darkness of errors and toward the destruction of the soul.

***

->**Arbatel of Magic**<-

**Sciences**
*   **Good**
    *   **Theosophy** <note>From the Greek *Theos* (God) and *Sophia* (Wisdom); the study of divine things and the will of God.</note>
        *   Knowledge of the Word of God, and ruling one’s life according to the word of God.
        *   Knowledge of the government of God by Angels, which the Scripture calls "watchmen"; and to understand the mystery of Angels.
    *   **Anthrosophy** <note>From the Greek *Anthropos* (Man) and *Sophia* (Wisdom); knowledge pertaining to human nature and the physical world.</note> **given to man**
        *   Knowledge of natural things.
        *   Wisdom in human affairs.
*   **Evil**
    *   **Cakosophy** <note>From the Greek *Kakos* (Bad/Evil) and *Sophia* (Wisdom); the perversion of knowledge or "wicked wisdom."</note>
        *   Contempt of the word of God, and living according to the will of the devil.
        *   Ignorance of the government of God by Angels.
        *   To despise the protection of the Angels, and to keep company with those of the devil.
        *   Idolatry.
        *   Atheism.
    *   **Cacodæmony** <note>From the Greek *Kakos* (Evil) and *Daimon* (Spirit); the study or practice of dealing with malevolent spirits.</note>
        *   The knowledge of poisons in nature, and how to use them.
        *   Wisdom in all evil arts for the destruction of mankind, and to use them in contempt of God, and for the loss and ruin of men.

## THE END.

<summary>This final page of the Arbatel provides a systematic classification of "Sciences" into good and evil categories, defining the difference between divine wisdom and destructive magic. It emphasizes that true wisdom (Theosophy) is rooted in the Word of God and the ministry of Angels.</summary>

--- Page 234 ---
<meta>This page concludes the introductory classification of the "Arbatel of Magic." It serves as a visual and textual summary, categorizing the different branches of human and spiritual knowledge into "Good" (Theosophy and Anthrosophy) and "Evil" (Cakosophy and Cacodæmony/Goetia).</meta>

# Astral Science <note>This refers to the knowledge of spiritual influences descending from the heavens.</note>

<margin>Theology</margin>
Knowledge of the Word of God, and directing one’s life according to the word of God. Knowledge of the Government of God by Angels, which the Scripture calls "watchmen" <note>A term for angels found in the biblical Book of Daniel, describing them as celestial observers and executors of God's decrees.</note>; and to understand the mystery of Angels.

<margin>Anthropology</margin>
Knowledge of natural things. Wisdom in human affairs.

---

<margin>Cacophy</margin>
<note>Original: *Cacosophia*. From the Greek *kakos* (evil) and *sophia* (wisdom); essentially "wicked wisdom" or the perversion of true knowledge.</note>
Contempt for the word of God, and living according to the will of the devil. Ignorance of the government of God by Angels. To consort with the nature of [fallen] angels, and their companions who belong to the devil. Idolatry. Astromancy <note>The practice of divination by the stars, here distinguished from the legitimate study of the heavens and viewed as a deceptive art.</note>.

<margin>Goetry</margin>
<note>Original: *Goëtia*. Derived from the Greek word for "wailing" or "shouting," referring to low magic, sorcery, or the conjuration of malevolent spirits.</note>
The knowledge of poisons in herbs, and how to use them. Wisdom in all evil arts intended for the destruction of mankind, and using them in contempt of God, and for the loss and ruin of men.

| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| | **Good** | **Theology** (Theosophy) |
| | | **Anthropology** (Anthrosophy) |
| | **Evil** | **Cacophy** (Cakosophy) |
| | | **Goetry** (Cacodæmony) |

<note>This hierarchical diagram serves as a map of the book's worldview, separating legitimate divine and natural study from the forbidden arts of the occult.</note>

->(THE END)<-

<summary>This concluding page of the introduction classifies all knowledge into four categories, distinguishing between divine/human wisdom and the destructive arts of "Cacophy" and "Goetry." It emphasizes that true wisdom comes from understanding God’s word and the role of his angelic "watchmen."</summary>
